# prorab Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. Tasks 11–12 additionally follow superpowers:writing-skills (RED → GREEN → REFACTOR with pressure scenarios).

**Goal:** A Claude Code skill `prorab` that lets one session (the foreman) run Claude crews in herdr panes across sites for hours without the customer, with work orders in beads, acceptance by evidence, control of context, limits and the dollar budget, handovers and a morning report.

**Architecture:** A lean English `SKILL.md` holds the rules; all mechanics live in small stdlib-Python scripts under `prorab/scripts/` (one responsibility each) that wrap the `herdr` and `bd` CLIs and read Claude transcripts. All numbers (thresholds, budget, prices, context windows) live in one file, `prorab/references/defaults.json`. State lives in the foreman's office's own beads database (the journal).

**Tech Stack:** Python 3 standard library only (`unittest` for tests), POSIX sh for the status-line tap, herdr ≥ 0.9.5, bd ≥ 1.3 (embedded Dolt), Claude Code 2.1.x, superpowers plugin (recommended, with fallback).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-prorab-design.md` (approved 2026-10-21; English rewrite with amendments S, L, G, R, E; amendment W folded in on 2026-10-22, amendment F on 2026-10-22 after the second live run, amendment M on 2026-10-22: the foreman's own lessons). Its glossary gives one English term per concept and the code identifier that carries it; this plan uses those terms and identifiers everywhere. Read the spec before any task; this plan argues from it, and where they disagree the spec wins.

**Amendments** (customer-approved 2026-10-21; short tags in the text): **S** — crew scheduling, the night reserve and the command "ignore limits and finish"; **L** — stale limits from idle sessions; **G** — patterns adopted from Gas City (ideas only: nothing is copied from Gas City code or fixtures; pattern labels P1–P16); **R** — estimates, spend and the shift budget in US dollars at API prices, counts only as machine protection; **E** — everything in English, identifiers too; **W** (customer-approved 2026-10-22) — the week by pace to its reset: a projection of the week at its reset, a working calendar and a reserve for the customer's own work instead of the fixed 50 % / 70 % (Task 12c); **F** (the foreman's orders after the second live run of Task 13, 2026-10-22) — the fixes F1–F8 of that run (Task 13b), the week projection by working hours (R101, Task 13b.7), the crew hooks decided "full" and the emergency stop (Task 14), and a short third live run (Task 13b.9); **M** (the customer, 2026-10-22) — the foreman's own lessons from this build: a timer for every time promised to the customer (monitor event `due`), the outcome of a consent action watched by the monitor (event `consent_done`), and rules on the panes the customer sees, grey prompt suggestions, short reports, delegation words, "hung" checked by facts and a changed budget unit (Task 14b). "Answer n" refers to the foreman's binding answers 1–5 of shift 2; R39–R52, R84–R88, R99–R101, R104 and R105 to the foreman's rulings.

**Rulings from the implementation** (the foreman's, made while Tasks 8b–8c ran; folded into the tasks below):

- **R45** — the composer is the line starting with `❯` (U+276F) directly between two horizontal-rule lines of `─` (U+2500); an empty composer is the bare `❯`; `>` is accepted as a fallback for older versions under the same rule; a choice-dialog line such as `❯ 1. Yes` is never a composer (verified on a live Claude Code pane).
- **R46** — `send` waits `agent prompt --wait --until working --until done --until blocked` (herdr's `--wait` gate already requires an observed `working` or `blocked` from a non-working state); after `stalled`, the single recovery Enter is followed by a wait `--until working --until blocked` only, because a standalone `agent wait` matches the current state and the pane was already `idle` or `done`: `working` → accepted, `blocked` → blocked, anything else or a timeout → `unconfirmed`; a wait output with no recognisable status falls back to `agent get`.
- **R47** — a reset zone in a limit message is resolved with `zoneinfo` (`UTC`, `GMT`, `Region/City`); an unresolvable zone gives an unknown reset (`None`), never local time.
- **R48** — a failed restart must not eat the restart cap; with it two report-check fixes: an empty item under "Changed files" is a rejection, never a crash, and the report's SHA must lie in `<base>..HEAD`.
- **R49** — the crew restart point is a successful `agent start`: from then on the session record names the new session and the restart counts toward the per-work-order restart limit, whatever the delivery result; a failure before `agent start` changes nothing.
- **R50** — in the monitor, a final provider error replaces `settled` for that crew's episode, like consent prompts and limit screens.
- **R51** — `crew.state` takes the already-read screen as `screen=` (an empty text is a read screen), so the monitor never reads a screen twice.
- **R52** — `crew.py check_report` rejects a stage report whose heavy-run count cannot be verified from records (no `--stage`, or the stage has no start time) with `heavy runs not verifiable`; the foreman always runs `check_report --stage <stage>` before `accept`, and `accept` itself does not check.

**Rulings of amendment W** (the foreman's, 2026-10-22; folded into Task 12c, with the author's choices on what they left open marked there):

- **R84** — `projected = used + burn × hours_to_reset + reserve`: `used` the measured week percentage (the max rule over fresh sources stays); the burn in pp per hour from sensor readings only, by the interval discipline of the rate (Task 8c.3: the same `seven_day_resets_at` within 10 min, never across a reset, at least 10 min and 1 pp apart), over a horizon in `defaults.json`; `hours_to_reset` from `seven_day.resets_at`; `reserve` = the customer's working hours left before the reset × `week.reserve_pp_per_hour`, on a working calendar in `defaults.json` (default Mon–Fri 09:00–19:00, the machine's local time; 1 pp per working hour) — both named as defaults; measuring the reserve from the customer's own sessions is later work.
- **R85** — `projected ≥ week.alert_pct` (95) → one notice to the customer (chat and the morning report; a push only under the existing urgent rules, and this is not one of the four urgent kinds) and no new mass runs; `≥ week.clearance_pct` (100) → only work orders with a clearance; `≤ 100 − week.margin_pp` (20) → the foreman may raise parallelism (still bounded by `parallel_ceiling`, the load bound and the 5h rules) or propose a night or weekend shift, never on its own, always within the shift budget in dollars and the 5h rules. These replace the fixed 50 % notify / 70 % clearance only.
- **R86** — no measured burn (no sensor, too few readings, a fresh week) or an unknown `resets_at` → the fixed thresholds stay as the fallback, named as such in the report (`week.fallback_notify_pct` 50, `week.fallback_clearance_pct` 70); an unknown week (`None`) keeps every existing rule (`blind_week` after 30 min; at night no new mass runs); "ignore limits and finish" lifts the projection exactly as it lifted the week thresholds.
- **R87** — the shift budget's +10 pp of the week stays: a spend ceiling per shift, not a week threshold.
- **R88** — the morning report shows the week used, the burn (pp/h, measured, or "fallback thresholds"), the projection at the reset, the reserve (working hours left × pp/h) and what the projection allows (normal / no new mass runs / clearance only / room for more).

**Rulings of amendment F** (the foreman's, 2026-10-22, shift 7; folded into Tasks 13b and 14, with the author's choices on what they left open marked "author's choice" there):

- **R99** — amendment F is one documents commit (the spec and this plan) for all of F1–F8 and Task 14 "full" with the emergency stop, reviewed before any code; Task 13b holds one step per fix, each fix one code commit (F8's items may share one); then the code commits in order.
- **R100** — the SKILL.md text of amendment F is drafted by opus only: cumulative drafts, one per code commit that touches SKILL.md, each at most 12 000 characters with the frontmatter and the section "Safety rules" byte-identical to the spec's; the implementers apply their draft verbatim. This plan names each SKILL.md change by reference only: what it must say and what may be cut.
- **R101** — the week projection has no separate reserve term: the measured working-hours burn already holds the customer's own use. `projected = used + B × W + N`: B the burn per working hour, W the working hours left before the reset, N the estimated pp of crew work scheduled outside working hours before the reset. Without a measured B or a reset time the R86 fallback stays; the levels R85/R92 are unchanged; the second live run's case (54 used, 1.745 pp per working hour, 18.66 working hours, nothing scheduled off-hours → 86.6 %, normal) is a test. R101 replaces the formula of R84, the reserve of R84 and R88, and the 24-hour horizon of Task 12c.
- **R104** — the pressure reruns that the SKILL.md sub-steps of Tasks 13b and 14 name are batched into one GREEN round 5 on the final SKILL.md, instead of one round per commit; by R105 that round runs after Task 14b's commit.

**Rulings of amendment M** (the foreman's, 2026-10-22, shift 7; folded into Task 14b, with the author's choices on what it left open marked "author's choice" there):

- **R105** — amendment M is one documents commit (the spec and this plan) and one code commit, Task 14b, after Task 14 and before the third live run (13b.9): the monitor events `due` and `consent_done` with their CLI and tests, the SKILL.md lines drafted by opus on top of Task 14's draft (R100) with their detail moved to a `references/` file, and the pressure scenarios S24 and S25. The third live run also sees `consent_done` on the F1 trust gate. GREEN round 5 (R104) runs after Task 14b's commit, on the final SKILL.md, with the RED baselines of S24 and S25 taken first on Task 14's SKILL.md; SKILL.md is cut once.

**State and execution order.** Tasks 1–8 (with the follow-up commits of S, L and the sensor) are done. Their text below describes the code as it will be after Task 8a — with the English identifiers — and their commit messages are given in the glossary terms (the actual commits carry the old names). The rest runs in this order, and the full suite passes after every commit:

1. **Task 8a** — English identifiers (one commit).
2. **Task 8b** — amendment G for the finished tasks: 8b.1 → 8b.8, one commit each, with answers 1–3 folded in.
3. **Task 8c** — amendment R for the finished tasks: 8c.1 → 8c.7, one commit each.
4. **Task 8d** — English strings for the finished code: 8d.1 → 8d.6, one commit each.
5. **Tasks 9 and 10**, in English from the start (R in Task 9).
6. **Stop point A** before Task 11: report to the foreman and wait for the go-ahead. Then **Task 10b** (shift 3: fast, unhangable tests and the stop-A defaults), then Tasks 11 and 12. **Stop point B** before Task 13: report and wait.
7. **Task 12c** — amendment W, the week by pace to its reset, after stop point B and before Task 13: the documents commit (the spec and this plan) is reviewed first, then Task 12c's one code commit, reviewed; the pressure scenarios that mention the week are rerun after it.
8. **Task 13** — install and the live smoke test, with the checks of answers 2, 3 and 5, and the week projection on real sensor data (check 16). Run twice (22 October); its Steps 4–6 (`notes.md` and the commit) wait for the third live run.
9. **Task 13b** — the fixes of the second live run (amendment F), after the documents commit of amendment F is reviewed: 13b.1 → 13b.8, one code commit each, in the order F1, F2, F3, F5, F7, F6, F4, F8.
10. **Task 14** — crew hooks, decided "full" by Task 13, with the emergency stop: one code commit.
11. **Task 14b** — amendment M, the foreman's lessons: after the documents commit of amendment M is reviewed, the RED baselines of S24 and S25 on Task 14's SKILL.md, then the opus draft of SKILL.md and `references/customer.md`, then one code commit; then GREEN round 5 (R104, R105).
12. **Task 13b.9** — the third live run, only for the fixed paths, the emergency stop and the hooks, with `consent_done` on the trust gate (Task 14b); it is also Task 14's live run (Step 6).
13. **Task 13 Steps 4–6** — `notes.md` from all three runs, the commit; then the final review of the whole branch, without push.

New strings written by Tasks 8b and 8c are English from the start; only strings that already exist wait for Task 8d.

## Global Constraints

- Required: herdr ≥ 0.9.5 with `HERDR_ENV=1`, bd ≥ 1.3. Nothing else is installed. prorab never initialises or edits the beads of a site.
- Python: standard library only; no pip, no uv, no third-party modules. Scripts start with `#!/usr/bin/env python3` and import siblings from `prorab/scripts/` (add `os.path.dirname(__file__)` to `sys.path`).
- Crews are Claude only (`--kind claude`). Codex is never started as a crew. There is no Russian-polish command: the key `polish_cmd` leaves `defaults.json` and the kv override `polish.cmd` is gone (R39, Task 8c.1).
- Journal: `BEADS_DIR=~/projects/prorab/.beads` for every `bd` call made by prorab. `.beads/` is in the repo `.gitignore`.
- No customer data in `prorab/`, `docs/` or the repo `CLAUDE.md`: no names of the customer's projects, no paths into them, no client data, no quotes from real sessions, no names of personal tools. Examples, fixtures and scenarios are synthetic and neutral only (e.g. `demo-app`, `/home/user/projects/demo-app`). Guarded by `prorab/tests/test_no_private_data.py`.
- Language (E): everything generated or shipped is English — `SKILL.md`, everything in `references/` (including the act and message templates sent to crews), the string values of `defaults.json`, every template the scripts render, work orders, stage acts, handover acts, customer gates, push notifications, the morning report, the monitor's event texts, strings and docstrings in scripts and tests (fixtures' expected texts included), the pressure scenarios, the trigger evals, the spec and this plan; commit messages too. Identifiers are English as well: module, function, variable and test names, CLI subcommands and flags, kv keys, bead labels, act kinds, event names, `defaults.json` keys, file and directory names take the glossary's terms, and no Russian word appears in them — neither in Cyrillic nor in Latin transliteration. Only the skill name `prorab` and names derived from it stay (`prorab:active`, `prorab:handoff`, `~/projects/prorab`, `~/.cache/prorab`, the branch `prorab/<id>`). Every trigger phrase in the `description` of `SKILL.md` appears in English and in Russian, plus `/prorab`; the trigger evals cover both languages, should-trigger and should-not-trigger alike. The foreman answers the customer in chat in whatever language the customer writes in; generated files stay English. The customer's Russian commands are described in English ("the customer's command 'ignore limits and finish', in any language"). One exception lives outside the repository (M, Task 14b): an approval act in the journal quotes the customer's delegation words verbatim, in the language they were written in, and no file in `prorab/` or `docs/` copies such a quote. Guards: no transliteration (Task 8a) and no Cyrillic outside the allowlist (Task 8d.6, R41, R43: the Russian column of the spec's glossary, the trigger phrases in the `description` of `SKILL.md`, and the files listed by name — the Russian test fixture and the trigger-eval file `prorab/evals/triggers.json`), both over `prorab/`, `docs/` and the repo `CLAUDE.md`.
- Thresholds (copy into `defaults.json` verbatim): context soft = min(40 % of the window, 500 000 tokens) (R40; it was 60 % until Task 8c.1); context hard = 80 % of the window; 5h day: 60 % no new mass runs, 80 % stop after the current step and wait for the reset; 5h night (night shift only): a window that resets before the morning → stop at 95 % (no separate no-new-mass threshold), the window that covers the morning → 70 % no new mass runs, 80 % stop (≥ 20 % left by the morning), reset time unknown → the day thresholds; morning `night.morning` = `08:00`; week (W, Task 12c; F, R101, Task 13b.7): by pace to its reset, `projected = used + B × W + N`, no reserve term — B the burn per working hour from sensor readings of the current week (each interval's rise counted in the share of its span that lies in working hours) over the last `week.burn_horizon_working_h` = 10 working hours, W the working hours of `week.calendar` = Mon–Fri 09:00–19:00 (the machine's local time) left before the reset, N the off-hours crew work (a night shift's budget left in pp of the week, else 0); `week.alert_pct` = 95 (one notice, no new mass runs: no new work order without a clearance), `week.clearance_pct` = 100 (only work orders with a clearance), `week.margin_pp` = 20 (at ≤ 80 room for more, nothing lifted); without a measured burn or a reset time the named fallback `week.fallback_notify_pct` = 50 (notify) and `week.fallback_clearance_pct` = 70 (only work orders with a clearance), never a margin case; the shift budget's +10 pp of the week stays (R87); unknown model window = 200 000; Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) window = 1 000 000.
- Budget (R): estimates, spend and the shift budget are counted in US dollars at Anthropic API list prices — a measure of work, not money paid (crews run on the Claude Max subscription). The cost of every assistant message follows the spec's formula from `message.usage`: input × input price, 5-minute cache writes × 1.25 × input price, 1-hour cache writes × 2 × input price, cache reads × cache-read price, output × output price, all per million tokens; usage without a 5m/1h split → the whole `cache_creation_input_tokens` at the 1-hour rate; every `message.id` counts once across all files read. The price table lives in `defaults.json` (`cost.prices`, input / output / cache read in dollars per million tokens: opus 4.00 / 20.00 / 0.20, sonnet 2.00 / 10.00 / 0.20, haiku 0.10 / 0.50 / 0.01, fable 10.00 / 50.00 / 0.25; the family is a substring of `message.model`); a model missing from the table is priced at the highest price in each column. A work order's spend covers every transcript of its crew, attributed by the crew's session ids recorded in the issue record, wherever their transcripts live (Claude Code moves a `-w` session's transcript to the site's project directory on exit, F6), and by the worktree (the `claude -p` runs started in it); their subagents count; one `message.id` counts once; the foreman's own research and one-off subagents run under their own dollar estimates; the foreman's own sessions count in the shift's spend on a line of their own. Default shift budget: $100 and +10 pp of the week (stop point A; it was $350). The rate ($ per pp) converts dollars into pp of the 5h window and of the week: estimated from the sensor only; until the first estimate the defaults 3.5 $/pp of the window and 10 $/pp of the week (stop point A; they were 5 $/pp and no week default), always named as defaults, never as estimates; the thresholds and the week projection are always checked against measured percentages, never against the rate. A work order over its estimate stops after its current step and waits for the customer with a gate, while the others go on; over the shift budget nothing new is issued and every crew stops after its current step, with a gate. Only "ignore limits and finish" lifts these two stops (R42).
- Machine protection — counts, not budget (R): no fixed crew count — the foreman takes the minimum of the ready tasks, the limit bound, the machine-load bound and `parallel_ceiling` = 6; several crews on one site only when their file lists are disjoint; `heavy_slots` = 2 heavy runs machine-wide; 60 heavy runs per stage unless the work order sets its own limit (stop point A; it was 20) (more needs a clearance); default expected duration 30 min. Sessions, subagents, tokens and agent runs are counted per work order for the report only, never as a limit.
- "Ignore limits and finish" (S, R42; the customer's command in any language; kv `override.ignore_limits`) lifts the 5h thresholds (the morning reserve included), the week thresholds (the week projection and its fallback thresholds, W), the shift budget and the work orders' estimates until the current goal is done; it never lifts the safety rules (consent prompts, push, PR, release, merge into main) or machine protection (the load bound, heavy slots, the heavy-run limit per stage, `parallel_ceiling`). It is the only exception to the "Never" list below, and only for its estimate-and-budget item.
- Limits from idle sessions are stale (L; Claude Code shows the last API response of THAT session; the countdown keeps running, the percent freezes): per window take the MAXIMUM over all panes / fresh per-session sensor files of the latest `resets_at`; a pane whose countdown shows `(0m)` belongs to the previous window and is ignored for it; an unknown week is `None`, never 0; unknown for > 30 min → `blind_week`; at night an unknown week means no new mass runs; an unknown week is never projected (W).
- Monitor heartbeat 20 min; a crew is "hung" = `working` with no transcript growth for 30 min **and** the newest file of its worktree older than 30 min, never while it holds a heavy slot; when a sign cannot be read the monitor reports it and the foreman never restarts on it (G); at most 2 restarts per work order — a limit screen and a handover never count as restarts (G); a restart counts from a successful `agent start`, whatever the delivery result, and a failure before it changes nothing and never counts (R48, R49).
- G numbers (copy into `defaults.json`, Task 8b.1): at most 6 approval and 10 review iterations per stage, 3 report-fix attempts; a second handover of one work order within 10 min is an incident; the monitor interval grows from 90 s to 300 s while nothing changes and nobody works.
- Never: answer a trust or consent prompt; press "Upgrade" (in "Stop and wait / Upgrade" only "Stop and wait" is ever pressed — G); re-send a prompt's text after `stalled` or `timeout` (G); press Enter on an empty composer (answer 3: Claude Code shows a grey prompt suggestion there, and Enter might submit it); push, open a PR, release, merge into main; exceed an estimate or the shift budget — the one item of this list that the customer's command "ignore limits and finish" lifts while it holds (R42); use `nohup`; delete a worktree with unmerged commits; press anything on the worktree exit dialog but the key `1` ("Keep worktree"), or confirm "Background work is running … 1. Exit and stop tasks" (F3); close or reuse a pane where an agent runs that is not the crew's own (F2, F7); write `~/.claude.json` (the trust check only reads it, F1); propose a work order on the foreman's office unless the customer orders one (F8); read a grey prompt suggestion as input, the customer's or a crew's (M); infer the customer's answer to a consent prompt beyond the signs of Task 14b — which option was chosen, or any outcome of a custom-API-key dialog — or resolve a consent gate without them (M). Every other item holds under that command too.
- Consent prompts (G): six kinds — `trust`, `mcp`, `claude_md_imports`, `bypass_warning`, `api_key`, `tool_permission` — are recognised in the bottom block of the screen only (`screen.tail_lines`; the same holds for limit screens, provider errors and the composer line), named in the gate and the morning report, and never answered; `api_key` means an urgent push to the customer. At most one push per gate or incident (`journal.mark_notified`); everything else goes to the morning report; no reminders. Folder trust is checked before every `-w` start by the exact site path in `~/.claude.json` (F1): without it, no `claude -w`, and the `trust` gate names the pane where the customer accepts it. The worktree exit dialog is not a consent prompt: only the script that sent the `/exit` answers it (F3). The gate on a consent prompt also records a watch, and the monitor reports the customer's answer in the pane as `consent_done` by fixed signs, never for `api_key` (M, Task 14b).
- Promised times and consent outcomes (M, Task 14b): a time the foreman promises the customer is a timer in the office journal's kv (`due.<label>`, `journal.py due`), raised by the monitor as `due <label>` once per timer and foreman pane; a consent watch is kv `consent.<work order>`, raised as `consent_done <work order> <kind>` once per watch and foreman pane and answered with `journal.py consent done`. Both come from the poll's one kv snapshot and agent list; the trust of a watched site is read from `~/.claude.json` in the monitor's process, read only, and only while a trust watch is open. No new process, no kv write by the monitor, no key pressed.
- herdr (F5): a herdr call whose answer the scripts do not read (`pane run`, `pane send-text`, `pane send-keys`, `agent send-keys`, `pane close`) goes through `herdr_cli.run_ack`, which takes any answer on exit 0 as success (empty, text, or JSON without an `error`) and raises only on a non-zero exit or a JSON `error`; every herdr failure names the command, its exit code and its error output; a crew script's failure result names the step that failed and the pane.
- Emergency stop (Task 14): while `~/.cache/prorab/STOP` exists, the crew hook denies every tool call (with hooks enabled), the monitor pauses every crew itself, once per crew, and raises `emergency_stop`, and `crew.py` refuses every command that starts a crew or sends to one, except `pause`; after the file is removed, crews are resumed only on the customer's word. One file test per monitor poll, no process.
- Text to a crew (G, answer 2): one line per prompt. Multi-line text (the work order, acts, fix lists) goes into a file under `send.files_dir` = `~/.cache/prorab/work-orders/` — the work order itself is `<id>.md` — and the crew gets one line naming it. Every crew is started with `--add-dir ~/.cache/prorab/work-orders`, so reading the file needs no permission outside the worktree (in bypass mode or not), and nothing is written into the site's worktree.
- Tests must never touch the real `~/.claude` (settings and the `projects/` transcripts), `~/.cache/prorab`, `~/projects/prorab/.beads`, `logs/` or real herdr panes; they use temp dirs, a temp `HOME`, `PRORAB_BEADS_DIR`, `PRORAB_CACHE`, `PRORAB_LOG_DIR` and the fake `herdr` stub. Every test that can reach `crew.message_file`, `send.files_dir` or any other `~/.cache/prorab` path (the work-order files and the `--add-dir` value, slots and their run log, the sensor's `usage/`, `heavy.count`, the events, the crew settings file and the emergency-stop file of Task 14) or `~/.claude.json` (the trust check of Task 13b.1 and the monitor's trust watch of Task 14b, which a test writes as a synthetic file under the temp `HOME`) sets a temp `HOME` and a temp `PRORAB_CACHE` before the defaults load (`_defaults.load` expands `~` from `HOME` at call time), and the tests that write such a file assert that it lies under the temp dir. Never run the status-line tap, `journal.py` or `scheduler.py plan` against real paths: they create real state. Only Task 13 and its third live run (Task 13b.9, which is also Task 14's live run, with the customer's consent for the hooks) touch real herdr and Claude.

## Review Focus

1. **No limit source at all** (clean install, no sensor, narrow panes): the monitor must report `blind` once, then stay quiet after the foreman acknowledges it (`kv limits.blind_ack`) — not wake the foreman every 90 s forever. Test in Task 7.
2. **A crew `idle` while background shells run** ("N shell" in its status line): not a stop point, no handover. Test in Task 4 (`bg_shells`) and Task 7 (`settled` suppressed).
3. **A crew waiting in a multiple-choice dialog** (`blocked`, but not a consent prompt): the foreman reads the options and answers within the work order; a consent prompt (trust, `.mcp.json`) is never answered. Test in Task 4 (`choice_dialog`) and Task 6 (`answer`). The stale-sensor case stays covered by Task 3's `test_sensor_stale_falls_through`. G: any of the six consent kinds is refused, and a tool-permission prompt is never parsed as a choice dialog (Task 8b.3).
4. **Prompt lost after startup** (`agent_prompt_stalled`): `send` returns `stalled`, never reports success; `timeout` with status `working` afterwards counts as accepted. Test in Task 4. G and answer 3: `send` waits `--until working --until done --until blocked`; after `stalled` exactly one Enter, only at `idle` or `done` (herdr shows `done` after a Claude turn) and only when the visible composer line (the `❯` line between two `─` rules, R45) holds the start of our text — never on an empty composer, where a grey prompt suggestion could be submitted; after that Enter only `working` (accepted) or `blocked` confirm: the wait is `--until working --until blocked`, since a standalone `agent wait` matches the `done` the pane already shows, and anything else or a timeout → `unconfirmed` (R46); the text is never sent twice; `timeout` while not `working` → `unconfirmed`, nothing pressed (Tasks 8b.3, 8b.6).
5. **A second foreman** started while one is alive: refuses (`acquire_active` false); a dead one's lock (pane gone from `herdr agent list`) is taken over. Test in Task 5.
6. **Night reserve**: night shift, 05:30, window at 78 % resetting at 10:00, 4 more tasks ready → no new mass runs (≥ 70), stop at 80, ≥ 20 % left at 08:00; unknown reset time at night → day thresholds. Test in Task 6b (`cap_for`) and Task 7 (`limit` uses `cap_for`).
7. **"Ignore limits and finish"**: no `limit` and no `budget` events while it holds, readings and spend still logged; machine protection still bounds `allowed_parallel`; the work orders' estimates are lifted with the budget, the heavy-run limit per stage is not (R42); it expires when every work order of the goal is accepted or waiting for the customer; W: it lifts the week projection too. Test in Tasks 5, 6b, 7, 8c.6, 8c.7, 12c.
8. **Stale limits from idle panes** (L): two panes 17.0 % (idle) and 28.0 % → 28; a `(0m)` pane at 70 % plus a fresh 5 % → 5; narrow panes only → week `None`; context = the first bar of the status line, not the last percent. Test in Task 3 (follow-up commit), Task 7 (`blind_week`), Task 8 (per-session files).
9. **A limit screen is not a failure** (G): no `bump_restarts`; in "Stop and wait / Upgrade" only "Stop and wait" is pressed and `answer` refuses every limit screen; a final provider error goes to the customer at once and replaces `settled` for that crew's episode (R50). Test in Tasks 8b.4, 8b.6, 8b.8.
10. **Consent kinds and pushes** (G): a marker above the bottom block is ignored; `bypass permissions on` in the status line is not a consent prompt; an `api_key` dialog raises one urgent push, and a monitor restart on the same screen raises none. Test in Tasks 8b.3, 8b.5, 8b.8.
11. **The stage report is checked by a script** (G): no commit, an empty branch or any git error → rejection; files outside the work order's "Files" → rejection; at most `cycles.report_fix` fix rounds, then a gate. R: a missing "Heavy runs" line, a reported number that differs from the heavy-run log of `slots.py`, or more heavy runs than the per-stage limit without a clearance → rejection; a count that cannot be verified from records (no `--stage`, or the stage has no start time) → rejection `heavy runs not verifiable`, never the crew's word (R52). Test in Tasks 8b.6, 8c.5.
12. **Monitor economy** (G): with a fresh sensor a steady poll starts exactly two processes (`herdr agent list`, `bd kv list`); `hung` needs both signs; a heavy-slot holder is never hung. R: the spend and the rate are computed by reading files inside the monitor's own process — never a process launch; W: so is the week's burn; F: so are the emergency-stop test (one file test per poll), the crews' `exiting` marks (from the kv snapshot) and, with hooks, the crews' event files; M: so are the timers and the consent watches (from the kv snapshot) and the trust of a watched site (a file read, only while a trust watch is open); the only deliveries a poll makes are the pauses of the first poll of an emergency stop, once per crew. Test in Tasks 8b.7, 8b.8, 8c.7, 12c, 13b.3, 13b.7, 14, 14b.
13. **Dollar cost from transcripts** (R): one `message.id` counts once across all files (streamed lines and forked or resumed sessions repeat ids); subagent transcripts and `claude -p` runs in the worktree count; sidechain lines count in the cost while the context still reads only the main chain; an unknown model is priced at the highest price of each column (never cheaper than any known model); usage without a 5m/1h split is priced as 1-hour writes; a sibling directory whose slug shares the worktree's prefix is not attributed. F6: see 26. Test in Tasks 8c.2, 13b.6.
14. **The rate** (R): only sensor readings, both ends of an interval in the same window (the same `resets_at` within 10 min, never across a reset), at least 10 min and 1 pp apart; rate = Σ$ / Σpp over the horizon; until the first estimate the defaults (3.5 $/pp for the window, 10 $/pp for the week; Task 10b.5), named as defaults; a week default set to null in `defaults.json` gives an unknown week rate (`None`), never 0; without the sensor no estimate at all. Test in Tasks 8c.3, 8c.7.
15. **Estimates and the budget in dollars** (R): a work order over its estimate → a `budget` event naming it, that work order stops after its step, the others go on; the shift over its budget → nothing new is issued; a research subagent whose estimate does not fit what is left is refused; the plan converts every estimate into pp by the rate before issuing. Test in Tasks 8c.4, 8c.6, 8c.7.
16. **Language guards** (E, R41): no banned transliteration anywhere in `prorab/`, `docs/` or `CLAUDE.md` (the guard builds its own word list from pieces so it does not trip itself); no Cyrillic outside the `description` of `SKILL.md`, the Russian column of the spec's glossary and the files the test lists by name (the Russian test fixture and the trigger-eval file, R43). Test in Tasks 8a, 8d.6, 12.
17. **The week by pace to its reset** (W, R84–R88; F, R101): `projected = used + B × W + N`, no reserve term. The regression case of the second live run, numbers verbatim: used 54, B 1.745 pp per working hour, W 18.66 working hours (Thu 14:20:24 → Mon 13:00, 94.66 hours to the reset), N 0 → 86.6 %, `normal` (the old formula gave 54 + 1.745 × 94.66 + 18.66, about 237.9 % with the unrounded measured B, `clearance_only`); and the synthetic one: 50 % used, B 1.2, W 20, N 0 → 74 %, `room`; with a night shift's 10 pp left → 84 %, `normal`. B: sensor readings only, the current week only (never across a reset; a week whose reset has passed is not current), intervals at least 10 min and 1 pp apart, each interval's rise counted in the share of its span that lies in working hours (a night or weekend interval counts nothing, one across 19:00 in proportion), those ending within the last `week.burn_horizon_working_h` working hours, over the working hours from the first counted interval's start to the latest reading — a quiet working stretch lowers B, a quiet night or crew work at night neither lowers nor raises it, and on a Monday morning the last working day still gives B. W: the working hours of `week.calendar` between now and the reset — a weekend, the hours outside 09:00–19:00 and a daylight-saving change count nothing extra. N: 0 without a night shift; with one, the lower of its week pp left and its dollars left at the week rate, never below 0. The levels: `≥ week.alert_pct` → no new work order without a clearance, one notice, no push; `≥ week.clearance_pct` → also the crews of uncleared work orders pause with a gate (`week.pause`); `≤ 100 − week.margin_pp` → room for more, which changes no bound and no budget; one notice per level per week. The fallback: no measured B or no reset time → `week.fallback_notify_pct` / `week.fallback_clearance_pct`, named as the fallback with its reason, never a margin case. An unknown week → no projection, every old rule (`blind_week`, the night's `cap_for(..., week_known=False)`). "Ignore limits and finish" → no `limit` event, every `fits.pace` true, `week.pause` empty. The morning report's week lines (Task 13b.7). Test in Tasks 12c, 13b.7.
18. **Trust by the exact site path** (F1): before any `claude -w`, `crew.start` reads `~/.claude.json` read only; only `projects[<the site path as given or its real path>].hasTrustDialogAccepted is True` counts — a trusted parent folder never does; a missing file, entry or flag → `needs_trust` naming the pane, with no `agent start`, no keys and no session id spent; a file that does not parse twice in a row → the start goes on, the screen refusal (`workspace trust not yet accepted`, a `trust` marker) as the backstop; the file is never written (bytes and mtime unchanged). Tests use a synthetic `~/.claude.json` under a temp `HOME`, never the real one. Test in Task 13b.1.
19. **The worktree exit dialog** (F3): answered only by the script that sent the `/exit` (`_exit`), only when the bottom block holds the dialog's marker and the line "1. Keep worktree", and only with `herdr pane send-keys <pane> 1`, at most once per exit — never `enter`, never `2`, never on words above the bottom block; with background shells no `/exit` is sent, and a "Background work is running" dialog gets one Esc, never `1`; `answer` refuses the dialog; while the crew is marked `exiting` (at most `crew.exit_window_s`) the monitor raises no event for it, not even `gone`. Test in Task 13b.3.
20. **No crew pane left empty, none taken from an agent** (F2, F7): every pane a start opens is recorded with its identity (herdr's `terminal_id` and `tab_id`), since pane ids can be reused; a pane whose identity no longer matches is forgotten, never reused or closed; a start reuses its own earlier pane only when its identity matches, it holds no agent and its shell stands in the site; a pane where another agent runs (the customer's `claude`) is never reused or closed while that runs; `stop` closes the crew's pane only after its agent is gone; `stop` and `cleanup` close the start's other recorded panes that hold no agent. Test in Tasks 13b.2, 13b.5.
21. **herdr's silent success** (F5): `herdr pane run` answers nothing on success; `run_ack` takes any answer on exit 0 as success (empty, text, or JSON without an `error`) and raises only on a non-zero exit or a JSON `error`, `run` keeps demanding JSON for the calls whose answer is read; every failure names the herdr command, its exit code and stderr; a restart and a handover survive the silent `cd` and reach `agent start`; their failure results name the step and the pane. The fake herdr answers silent commands with an empty body, as the real one does. Test in Task 13b.4.
22. **A changed work order on resume** (F7): a `--resume` restart sends the work-order file in one line (the `Changed work order` block) when the work-order text's fingerprint differs from the one recorded at the crew's last accepted delivery, or none is recorded; else "Continue"; the text is never sent twice in one restart. Test in Task 13b.5.
23. **Takeover by records** (F8c): `shift.py handoff` returns `handed_over` only when the lock names the new pane, a `reconcile` by the new pane is recorded since the handoff began and a monitor mark of the new pane started since then exists and its pid was seen alive during the wait; never by reading the new pane's screen; the monitor writes its mark without a process start. Test in Task 13b.8.
24. **The crew guard and the settings file** (Task 14): `--settings` only while kv `hooks.enabled` is `1`; the file holds only `hooks`, `feedbackSurveyRate` and `promptSuggestionEnabled`, lives in the cache, and never holds `permissions`, `defaultMode`, `enableAllProjectMcpServers` or `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`; the guard denies every `hooks.deny` entry, the office `BEADS_DIR`, a merge or rebase on `main`/`master` (the repository found by walking up from the command's directory, a worktree's `gitdir:` followed; a branch it cannot read counts as forbidden), and any input it cannot parse (exit 2, a reason on stderr), and starts no git process; its matching is text, not a boundary (`git -C x push` passes the `git push` entry), so the hooks back the work order and acceptance and never replace them; hooks never answer a consent prompt. Test in Task 14.
25. **The emergency stop** (Task 14): while `~/.cache/prorab/STOP` exists, the crew's PreToolUse entry with matcher `*` denies every tool, not only Bash (a one-line shell test, no Python, no file write); the monitor's first poll that sees the file pauses every listed crew once and raises `emergency_stop`, later polls raise nothing for crews, limits or budget; `crew.py` refuses every start and send but `pause`; removing the file raises `emergency_released` once and resumes nothing by itself. Test in Task 14; seen live in Task 13b.9.
26. **Spend after a worktree exit** (F6): Claude Code moves a `-w` session's transcript from the worktree's project directory to the site's on exit, and a resumed session keeps writing there. A work order's spend still covers it: every crew session id of the work order is kept in the issue record (the first start, each handover, each restart with a new session; never twice; a `--resume` adds none), and each id's transcript is found wherever it lives — the worktree's directory, then the site's, only then the rest — with its subagents beside it; the worktree's own directories still give the `claude -p` runs. The site's project directory is never read as a whole, so the customer's own sessions there never count; a message found both ways counts once; the sibling-slug rule of 13 holds. `crew.work_order_spend`, `journal.shift_spend`, the monitor's budget check and the morning report use the one function `transcript.work_order_files`. The live case: $0.00 by the worktree alone against $0.539 for the moved session. Test in Task 13b.6.
27. **Promised times** (M): `journal.py due set` keeps a timer in kv `due.<label>`; the monitor raises `due <label>` on the first poll at or after its time, once per timer and foreman pane (kv `prorab:active`) — never before its time, never twice for one foreman, once more for a foreman that took over before the timer was cleared; a cleared timer raises nothing, and one set again for a new time fires again; the wait before a poll never runs past the next timer, whatever the backoff; `due` fires under "ignore limits and finish" and during an emergency stop; a record that does not parse is a failing source, never a crash. Test in Task 14b.
28. **Consent outcomes** (M): the gate on a consent prompt (`journal.py wait … "consent prompt: <kind>"`) records the watch kv `consent.<work order>` only when the journal knows the pane, and never for `api_key`; the monitor raises `consent_done <work order> <kind>` once per watch and foreman pane — for `trust` only when `crew.site_trust(site)` is `True` (read only, in-process, only while a trust watch is open; an unreadable file is "not yet") and no agent is listed in the watched pane; for a dialog only when the crew's own agent, by name, is listed in the watched pane, its status is not `blocked`, and its screen, when read this poll, shows no consent prompt; nothing for an agent gone from the pane, for a foreign agent, while the emergency stop holds or while the crew is marked `exiting` (it fires once they end, if the signs still hold); `journal.py consent done` resolves that gate, clears the watch and records the act. The monitor presses nothing, writes no kv and starts no process. Test in Task 14b.

## File Structure

```
~/projects/prorab/
  CLAUDE.md                      2 lines: this is the foreman's office; load the prorab skill
  .gitignore                     .beads/ logs/ __pycache__/ *.pyc *.gate.lock reports/ (F8)
  prorab/
    SKILL.md                     rules only (Task 12)
    references/
      defaults.json              every number and the price table (Task 1, 8b.1, 8c.1, 12c)
      work-order.md              work-order template (Task 10)
      acts.md                    act templates and crew messages (Task 6, 8b.6, 8d.1, 10, 14b)
      customer.md                the foreman with the customer: promised times, consent outcomes, panes,
                                 grey suggestions, reports, delegation words, "hung" checked, budget units
                                 (Task 14b, M)
      superpowers.md             process mapping + fallback procedure (Task 10)
      passport.md                site passport template (Task 10, G)
      notes.md                   verified herdr/Claude quirks found in Task 13
    scripts/
      _defaults.py               load defaults.json
      transcript.py              Claude transcripts: find, context, last text, usage, cost in dollars (8c.2)
      limits.py                  Claude Max limits: sensor → status line → messages; the dollar-to-pp rate (8c.3);
                                 the week's burn, the working calendar and the week projection (12c)
      herdr_cli.py               herdr wrapper: run, env check, agents, visible text, send, bg shells, composer
      journal.py                 bd wrapper: work orders, stages, acts, kv, budget and spend, lock, gates;
                                 timers of promised times and consent watches (14b)
      crew.py                    start/send/state/summary/handover/stop/accept (CLI + functions)
      scheduler.py               critical-path order, allowed parallel crews, day/night 5h caps, window consumption speed, fit (CLI)
      slots.py                   machine-wide flock semaphore for heavy runs (CLI, used by crews)
      monitor.py                 one-shot event loop (CLI)
      sensor.py                  install/uninstall/status of the status-line tap (CLI)
      statusline_tap.sh          the tap itself
      shift.py                   night-shift preflight, caffeinate, foreman handover, morning report (CLI)
      hooks.py                   crew settings file, guard, emergency-stop check and event tap via --settings
                                 (Task 14, "full")
    tests/
      fakes/herdr                stub executable driven by FAKE_HERDR_DIR
      fixtures/                  transcript .jsonl samples (cost/ with subagents and forks, 8c.2),
                                 status-line text with U+00A0; consent/, composer/ and limit screens
                                 (synthetic, G); choice_dialog_cyrillic.txt (the one Cyrillic fixture, 8d.4);
                                 exit/ (the worktree exit and background-work dialogs, 13b.3)
      test_*.py                  one per script, plus test_language.py (8a, 8d.6) and test_no_private_data.py
                                 test_timeouts.py (10b.1), test_run.py (10b.4)
      run.py                     bounded, parallel test runner; --fast skips the bd-backed modules (10b.4)
    evals/
      pressure/                  scenarios + baseline/after results (Tasks 11–12)
      triggers.json              should- and should-not-trigger prompts, English and Russian (Task 12;
                                 listed by name in the Cyrillic guard, R43)
  reports/                       morning reports (git-ignored: they hold the customer's paths and project
                                 data, F8)
  logs/                          monitor log (ignored)
  docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/
~/.cache/prorab/                 outside the repo: usage/, slots/, work-orders/ (work orders and multi-line
                                 texts for crews, G, answer 2), events/ and crew-settings.json (Task 14), STOP
                                 (the customer's emergency stop, created and removed by the customer or on the
                                 customer's word; unit tests touch only a temp cache's)
```

Run all tests: `cd ~/projects/prorab && python3 -m unittest discover -s prorab/tests -t . -v` (serial), or bounded and in parallel: `python3 prorab/tests/run.py` (Task 10b.4). TDD loops use the fast tier, which never starts the real `bd`: `PRORAB_FAST=1 python3 -m unittest <focused modules>` or `python3 prorab/tests/run.py --fast`; the full suite runs once before each commit and at stop points (Task 10b).

### Task 3: Claude Max limits

**Files:**
- Create: `prorab/scripts/limits.py`, `prorab/tests/test_limits.py`, `prorab/tests/fixtures/statusline_wide.txt` (contains `5h ▓░░░░░░░░░ 17.0% (4h7m) │ wk ▓▓░░░░░░░░ 26.0% (4d21h7m)`), `prorab/tests/fixtures/statusline_narrow.txt` (truncated after `5h ▓░░…`)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `Limits` = dict `{"five_hour": float|None, "seven_day": float|None, "five_hour_resets_at": int|None, "seven_day_resets_at": int|None, "five_hour_resets_in_s": int|None, "source": "sensor"|"statusline"|"messages"|"unknown", "reached": bool}` (`five_hour_resets_in_s`: from the sensor as `resets_at - now`, from the status line as the countdown, else `None`).
  - `read_sensor(path: str, max_age_s: int, now: float | None = None) -> Limits | None` — reads `rate_limits.five_hour.used_percentage`, `.resets_at`, the same for `seven_day`; `None` if missing, unparsable or `mtime` older than `max_age_s`.
    L (follow-up commit): `path` is the per-session directory `limits.sensor_dir` (`<session_id>.json` files written by Task 8's tap); per window, take the files fresher than `max_age_s`, group by `resets_at` (10-minute tolerance, Task 8's follow-up), keep the group with the latest `resets_at` and its maximum `used_percentage`; `None` when no file is fresh. `prune_sensor(path, retention_s, now=None)` deletes regular files older than `limits.sensor_retention_s` (the reader prunes; the tap only writes).
  - `parse_statusline(text: str) -> Limits | None` — regexes `r"5h\s+\S+\s+([\d.]+)%"`, `r"wk\s+\S+\s+([\d.]+)%"` (`\s` must match U+00A0); also the countdown after the 5h percent, `(4h7m)` style (`d`, `h`, `m` parts, any subset) → `five_hour_resets_in_s` (S, follow-up commit). The reset time from Claude Code limit messages is not parsed until Task 13 records the real wording; until then it is unknown.
  - `detect_messages(text: str, patterns: list[str]) -> bool` — any pattern, case-insensitive.
  - `get_limits(pane_texts: list[str], d: dict, now: float | None = None) -> Limits` — sensor, else first pane that parses, else messages (`reached` True, percentages None), else `unknown`.
    L (follow-up commit): instead of the first pane, parse every pane and take, per window, the MAXIMUM percent; a pane whose countdown next to that window's percent is `(0m)` is skipped for that window (a truncated countdown still counts); `five_hour_resets_in_s` from the pane that gave the maximum, else the smallest positive countdown seen; no week in any pane → `seven_day` = `None`.
  - `statusline_context(text: str) -> float | None` — the percent of the FIRST bar on the status line (the session's context), never a later bar such as 5h (L). Audit every place that reads a context percent from the screen and apply the same rule.
  - CLI: `limits.py [--pane-text-file F ...]` prints `Limits` JSON; without files it reads all agent panes via `herdr_cli.pane_visible` (Task 4) — imported lazily so Task 3 tests need no herdr.
  - G (Task 8b.4): `limit_screen`, `provider_error`, `parse_resets` ("resets …" is now parsed); messages are matched in the bottom block only; `get_limits` reads pane texts lazily.
  - R (Task 8c.3): `rate_intervals`, `estimate_rate`, `rates`, `to_pp` — the dollar-to-pp rate from sensor readings.
  - W (Task 12c): `week_burn`, `working_hours`, `week_projection`, `WEEK_LEVELS`; CLI `limits.py week`.

### Task 4: herdr wrapper and reliable send

**Files:**
- Create: `prorab/scripts/herdr_cli.py`, `prorab/tests/fakes/herdr` (executable), `prorab/tests/test_herdr_cli.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Fake: `tests/fakes/herdr` (Python) maps `argv[1:]` joined by spaces to a response file in `$FAKE_HERDR_DIR` via `$FAKE_HERDR_DIR/routes.json` (`{"<argv prefix>": "<file>"}`, longest prefix wins; `{"seq": [...]}` answers in turn); appends every call to `$FAKE_HERDR_DIR/calls.log`; exit code from the route (`{"file": ..., "exit": 1}`). Tests put `tests/fakes` first on `PATH`.
- Produces:
  - `class HerdrError(Exception)` with `.code: str`.
  - `run(args: list[str], timeout_s: int = 60) -> dict` — runs `herdr`, parses JSON; a JSON `{"error": {"code": ...}}` raises `HerdrError(code)`.
  - `check_env() -> None` — raises `HerdrError("not_in_herdr")` unless `HERDR_ENV == "1"`; parses `herdr --version` (`herdr 0.9.5` style) and raises `HerdrError("herdr_too_old")` below 0.9.5.
  - `agents() -> list[dict]`, `agent(name: str) -> dict | None`, `pane_visible(pane_id: str) -> str`, `agent_visible(name: str) -> str` (`--source visible`).
  - `send_keys(name: str, keys: list[str]) -> None` — `agent send-keys NAME KEY ...` (logical keys: `enter`, `esc`, `up`, `down`, digits).
  - `choice_dialog(visible: str) -> list[str] | None` — when the visible text contains `Enter to select` and `↑/↓ to navigate` (Claude's multiple-choice question), returns the option labels in order (lines matching `r"^\s*(?:❯\s*)?(\d+)\.\s+(.*)$"` in the dialog block only, follow-up fix), else `None`; `choice_state(visible)` also returns the highlighted index.
  - `bg_shells(visible: str) -> int` — max of `r"· (\d+) shells?"` and `r"(\d+) shells? still running"`, 0 if none.
  - `send(name: str, text: str, timeout_ms: int = 15000) -> str` — `agent prompt NAME TEXT --wait --timeout MS`; returns `"accepted"` on success or on `timeout` when `agent(name)["agent_status"] == "working"` afterwards; `"stalled"` on `agent_prompt_stalled`; `"blocked"` on `agent_blocked`; re-raises anything else.
  - G (Task 8b.3) and answer 3: `send` waits `--until working --until done --until blocked` (60 s) and returns `unconfirmed` instead of raising on a non-working `timeout`; new `recover_stalled` (one Enter at `idle` or `done`, only when the composer holds the start of our text, then a wait for `working` or `blocked` only — R46), `composer_text` (the `❯` line between two rules, R45), `wait_ready`, `bottom_block`, `consent_dialog` with `CONSENT_KINDS` and `CONSENT_NAMES`, `valid_name`, `agent_by_pane`; `choice_state` is `None` on any consent prompt.

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `_defaults.load()`, `herdr_cli.agents()` (for lock liveness).
- Produces (every call sets `BEADS_DIR`; default `~/projects/prorab/.beads`, overridable by `PRORAB_BEADS_DIR` for tests):
  - `bd(args: list[str], json_out: bool = True) -> dict | list | str`.
  - `ensure_db() -> None` — `bd init --non-interactive --skip-agents --skip-hooks --prefix prorab` in the foreman's office if `.beads` is missing.
  - `create_work_order(site: str, title: str, body: str, estimate: dict) -> str` — labels `prorab:work-order,site:<site>` (`WORK_ORDER_LABEL`); stores the estimate in kv `estimate.<id>.<key>` (free keys until Task 8c.4 fixes them to `usd` and `heavy_runs_per_stage`).
  - `create_stage(parent: str, title: str, body: str, blocked_by: list[str] = [], files: list[str] = [], duration_min: float | None = None, kind: str | None = None) -> str` — `--parent`, `--deps blocked-by:<id>,...`; `files` and `duration_min` (the expected duration) stored in the bead's metadata, `kind` as label `type:<kind>` (S).
  - `create_work_order` also takes `goal: str | None = None` → label `goal:<goal>` (S §4).
  - `stage_info(stage: str) -> dict` — `{"files", "duration_min", "kind", "started_at", "actual_min"}`; `work_order_files(work_order: str) -> list[str]` — union of its stages' files; `start_stage(stage: str) -> None` — status `in_progress`, records `started_at`; `accept_stage(stage: str, act: str) -> float` — act `acceptance`, records and returns the actual duration in minutes, closes the stage; `durations(kind: str) -> list[float]` — actual durations of closed stages of that kind (S).
  - `set_override(goal: str) -> None` — kv `override.ignore_limits` = JSON `{"goal", "since", "source": "customer's words"}`; `override() -> dict | None` — the stored value, or `None` (and the key cleared) once the goal has at least one work order and every work order of the goal is closed or waiting for the customer (a stage blocked only by waiting stages waits too — follow-up fix); `clear_override() -> None` (S §4).
  - `ready() -> list[dict]` — `bd ready --json` filtered to label `prorab:work-order` or children of one; for the scheduler (S): `graph()`, `open_stages(work_order)`, `working_work_orders()`, `ready_work_orders()`.
  - `add_act(bead: str, kind: str, text: str) -> None` — a comment starting with `[act:<kind>]`; kinds `acceptance`, `handover`, `shift`, `answer`.
  - `acts(bead: str, kind: str | None = None) -> list[str]`.
  - `kv_get(key: str) -> str | None`, `kv_set(key: str, value: str) -> None`, `kv_clear(key)`, `kv_all()`.
  - `budget() -> dict` — `defaults["budget"]` overlaid with kv `budget.<key>`; `set_budget(**kw) -> None`.
  - `spend(work_order: str, sessions: int = 0, runs: int = 0, tokens: int = 0) -> None` (adds to `spent.<id>.*` and `shift.spent.*`); `shift_spent() -> dict`; `over_budget(week_pp_now: float | None) -> list[str]` (names of exceeded items).
  - `acquire_active(pane_id: str) -> bool` — kv `prorab:active`; free, own, handed off to us (`prorab:handoff`), or held by a pane missing from `herdr_cli.agents()` → take it; else False.
  - `wait_customer(bead: str, reason: str) -> str` — `bd gate create` (type `human`) blocking the bead + act `answer` with the reason; returns the gate id.
  - `bump_restarts(work_order: str) -> int` — kv `restarts.<id>` + 1, returns the new value.
  - `crews() -> list[str]`, `register_crew(name: str) -> None`, `unregister_crew(name: str) -> None` — kv `crews.active` (comma list).
  - `begin_shift(week_pct: float | None) -> None` — resets `shift.spent.*`, stores `shift.week_start` and a snapshot of `budget()`.
  - CLI `journal.py` with subcommands mirroring the functions (`ensure`, `work-order`, `stage`, `stage-info`, `stage-start`, `stage-accept`, `work-order-files`, `durations`, `override get|set|clear`, `ready`, `graph`, `ready-work-orders`, `open-stages`, `act`, `acts`, `budget [--set k=v ...]`, `spend`, `spent`, `over`, `shift-begin`, `lock`, `wait`, `restarts`, `crews`, `kv get|set`), JSON out — this is how the foreman's session drives the journal.
  - G (Task 8b.5, answer 1): cycle counters (P5), `mark_notified` (P10), the site passport (P11), issue records `issuing` / `issued` / `not_issued` (P13), a `kv` snapshot keyword for the monitor (P8).

### Task 6: Crew lifecycle

**Files:**
- Create: `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`, `prorab/references/acts.md` (the fixed texts the scripts send; each read as the first fenced block under its heading)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `herdr_cli.*`, `transcript.find_transcript/context_status/last_text/usage_totals`, `journal.add_act/acts/spend`, `_defaults.load()`.
- Produces (functions + CLI subcommands of the same names, JSON out):
  - `start(site_path: str, work_order: str, name: str, caller_pane: str) -> dict` — checks: `len(journal.crews()) < budget()["parallel_ceiling"]` (`machine()` from Task 8c.1; else `{"status": "parallel_limit"}`; the real number is decided before the call by `scheduler.allowed_parallel`); no registered crew on the same site (kv `crew.<name>.site`, else its `cwd`, under `site_path`) whose work-order files (`journal.work_order_files`) intersect this work order's files — an empty file list on either side counts as intersecting (else `{"status": "files_overlap", "with": [names]}`) (S); splits the caller pane (`--direction right` if its width ≥ 2 × height in `pane layout`, else `down`, `--no-focus`, `--cwd site_path`); `agent start NAME --kind claude --pane P -- -w prorab-<work_order>`; on `agent_not_ready` with a consent screen → `{"status": "needs_trust" | "needs_consent", "pane_id": P}` (never sends keys); otherwise finds the worktree via `git -C site_path worktree list --porcelain` (path ending in `prorab-<work_order>`) and renames its branch to `prorab/<work_order>` if different; registers `name` and kv `crew.<name>.work_order`, `.site`, `.pane`, `.worktree`, `.branch`; returns `{"status": "started", "pane_id", "name", "session_id", "worktree", "branch"}` (`started_no_worktree` when none was found) and records them as act `answer` on the work order.
  - `send_work_order(name: str, text: str) -> str` — `herdr_cli.send`; `"stalled"` → one retry after 5 s, then return `"stalled"` (Task 8b.6 removes the retry).
  - `state(name: str) -> dict` — `{"status", "bg_shells", "ctx": context_status | None, "session_id", "cwd", "pane_id"}`; `{"status": "gone", ...}` when the agent is missing.
  - `summary(name: str) -> str` — `last_text` of its transcript.
  - `request_handover(name: str) -> str` — sends the fixed request under `## Handover request` in `references/acts.md` (finish the step, no new runs, write the act, answer `HANDOVER READY`).
  - `complete_handover(name: str, work_order: str, work_order_text: str) -> dict` — requires `state.bg_shells == 0` and a `HANDOVER READY` line; reads the act (its last text minus the marker line) → `journal.add_act(work_order, "handover", act)` and `journal.spend(work_order, sessions=1, tokens=<tokens of the closed session>)` before anything else (8c.5 drops `tokens` here: the dollars come from the worktree's transcripts); sends `/exit`; waits until `agent(name)` is gone and the pane shows a shell; `cd <worktree>` in the pane (shell-quoted; an unknown worktree → `restart_failed`, never the site directory) and waits until the shell is there; `agent start NAME --kind claude --pane P` (no `-w`); sends `work_order_text` + all `handover` acts. Consent screens and an unaccepted prompt return their own statuses; nothing is retried blindly.
  - `evidence(worktree: str, base: str = "main") -> dict` — compact facts for acceptance: `branch`, `commits` (`git log --oneline base..HEAD`), `diffstat`, `dirty` (porcelain non-empty).
  - `merge_check(worktree: str, accepted: list[str]) -> dict` — read-only: for the site's branches accepted earlier in the same goal (in acceptance order) reports `missing` (branches not yet contained, `git merge-base --is-ancestor`), `conflicts` (`git merge-tree --write-tree` against each missing one) and `errors`; the foreman never merges — a crew merges or rebases by work order, nothing goes to main (S).
  - `answer(name: str, choice: int | None = None, text: str | None = None) -> str` — a consent screen → `"consent"`, nothing sent; if a choice dialog is open: `choice` (1-based) → move from the highlighted option with `down`/`up` and `enter`; `text` → select the "Type something" option, type the text, `enter`. Without a dialog: `herdr_cli.send(name, text)`. Records the answer as act `answer` on the current work order. Returns the new agent status.
  - `pause(name: str) -> str` — sends the fixed pause text under `## Pause` in `references/acts.md` (finish the current step, start nothing, wait).
  - `stop(name: str, work_order: str) -> None` — `/exit`; `unregister_crew`; `journal.spend(work_order, tokens=input+cache_creation+output from usage_totals)`; clears the kv `crew.<name>.*` fields; leaves the worktree.
  - `cleanup(site_path: str, work_order: str) -> dict` — removes worktree `prorab-<work_order>` only if branch `prorab/<work_order>` is merged into the default branch (`git branch --merged`) or the customer recorded a drop decision (an act `answer` starting with `DROP_MARKER` = `[customer decision] drop`, the block under `## Customer decision to drop the branch` in `acts.md`); else `{"status": "kept", "reason": "unmerged"}`.
  - G (Task 8b.6, answers 2 and 3): `send_work_order`'s text retry is removed (`deliver`: one Enter after `stalled` only at `idle`/`done` with our text in the composer; multi-line by file); `start` records `issuing` first and passes `--session-id` and `--add-dir ~/.cache/prorab/work-orders`; new `name_for`, `message_file`, `deliver`, `wait_limit`, `resume`, `restart`, `check_report`, `report_fix`, `ask_predecessor`; the handover act gets git facts.
  - R (Task 8c.1, 8c.5): the ceiling from `journal.machine()`; the worktree in the issue record; `work_order_spend`, `accept`, the "Heavy runs" line in `check_report`; spend in dollars at acceptance, handover and stop; `ask_predecessor` only inside the worktree.

### Task 6b: Scheduler and heavy-run slots (S)

**Files:**
- Create: `prorab/scripts/scheduler.py`, `prorab/scripts/slots.py`, `prorab/tests/test_scheduler.py`, `prorab/tests/test_slots.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `_defaults.load()`, `journal.ready/stage_info/durations/crews/budget/override/kv_get/graph/open_stages/ready_work_orders`, `limits.get_limits`, monitor log readings (Task 7 writes them; until then the CLI tolerates a missing log).
- Produces:
  - `critical_order(graph: dict[str, list[str]], durations: dict[str, float]) -> list[str]` — `graph[id]` = ids that block `id`; remaining path of `id` = `durations[id]` + the longest remaining path among the tasks `id` blocks (0 if none); returns every id sorted by remaining path, longest first, ties by id.
  - `duration(stage: dict, history: dict[str, list[float]], d: dict) -> float` — the expected duration: `stage["duration_min"]`, else the median of `history[stage["kind"]]`, else `d["schedule"]["default_duration_min"]`; `work_order_durations(j, d, ids)` — a work order's expected duration = the sum over its open stages.
  - `burn_rate(readings: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> float | None` — the window's consumption speed: readings `(unix_ts, five_hour_pct)` in time order; uses only the readings after the last drop (a reset); percentage points per hour over that span; `None` with fewer than 2 such readings or a span shorter than `schedule.burn_min_span_s`. (Not the dollar rate of Task 8c.3.)
  - `cap_for(now: float, resets_at: float | None, morning: str, d: dict, night: bool = False) -> dict` — `{"mode": "day"|"night_before_morning"|"night_morning_window", "no_new_mass": float | None, "stop": float}`; morning = the next local `HH:MM` after `now`; day thresholds unless `night` and `resets_at` is known; `resets_at <= morning` → `night_before_morning` (`no_new_mass` None, `stop` 95); else `night_morning_window` (70 / 80). `night` = kv `shift.night` == "1" (set by Task 9's night `begin`). L: keyword `week_known: bool = True`; at night with `week_known` False, `no_new_mass` = 0 (no new mass runs).
  - `allowed_parallel(ready: int, burn_pp_per_h: float | None, used_pct: float | None, cap_pct: float, resets_in_s: int | None, active: int, load1: float, ncpu: int, d: dict) -> int` — total crews allowed at once (active included) = `max(0, min(active + ready, limit_bound, load_bound, parallel_ceiling))`; `limit_bound`: unknown usage → `max(1, active)` (never treated as zero — follow-up fix); 0 if `used_pct >= cap_pct`; no bound (= ceiling) when `burn_pp_per_h` is None or `active == 0`; else `floor((cap_pct - used_pct) / ((burn_pp_per_h / active) * hours))`, `hours` = `resets_in_s / 3600` or 5 when unknown; `load_bound` = `active` when `load1 > ncpu`, else the ceiling. `cap_pct` is the `stop` of `cap_for` (100 while "ignore limits and finish" holds).
  - `machine_load() -> tuple[float, int]` — `sysctl -n vm.loadavg` (first number) and `sysctl -n hw.ncpu`.
  - CLI `scheduler.py plan` → JSON `{"order": [...ready ids by critical path], "allowed": n, "active": k, "new": n - k, "cap": {...}, "override": {...} | null, "limits_unknown": bool}` — this is what the foreman calls before issuing; `scheduler.py cap` → the current `cap_for` JSON.
  - `slots.run(kind: str, cmd: list[str], slots_dir: str | None = None, n: int | None = None, poll_s: float = 1.0) -> int` — `n` lock files `<slots_dir>/<kind>.<i>.lock`, `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)` on the first free one, else sleep `poll_s` and retry; runs `cmd` with inherited stdio, returns its exit code; the lock is released when the command ends (also if the process dies). `slots_dir` = `$PRORAB_CACHE/slots` (default `~/.cache/prorab/slots`); `n` = the number in `<slots_dir>/<kind>.count` if present (Task 9 writes it from `journal.machine()["heavy_slots"]` at shift start), else `heavy_slots` from `defaults.json`. Crews never read or write the journal.
  - CLI `slots.py run --kind heavy -- <cmd...>` (exit code of the command) and `slots.py status` (busy slots as JSON).
  - G (Task 8b.7): an owner record next to each held slot (`slots.holders`); `gather_limits` reads pane texts only when the sensor gives nothing.
  - R (Task 8c.1, 8c.6): the ceiling from `machine` (kv-overlaid in the plan); `fits` — every estimate in pp by the rate, checked against the shift budget and the thresholds before issuing.
  - W (Task 12c): `fits` gains `pace`; `build_plan` adds `week` (the projection and `pause`); `cleared`; `cap_for` and `allowed_parallel` unchanged.

### Task 7: Monitor

**Interfaces:**

- Produces:
  - `poll(crews: list[str], d: dict, mem: dict, now: float, *, deps=None) -> list[str]` — event lines, in priority order: `gone <name>`, `limit <5h> <wk> <source> <mode>` (5h ≥ `cap_for(...)["no_new_mass"]` when set or ≥ its `stop`, or wk ≥ 50, or `reached`; `cap_for` from Task 6b with the reset time from the limits and the night flag from kv `shift.night`) — Review Focus 6, `blind` (source `unknown` twice in a row **and** kv `limits.blind_ack` unset) — Review Focus 1, `blind_week` (week `None` for longer than `limits.week_unknown_blind_s`, once per streak, silenced by kv `limits.blind_week_ack`; L) — Review Focus 8, `budget <items>` (the set of exceeded budget items changed; dollars from Task 8c.7), `prorab_context <pct>`, `context_hard <name> <pct>`, `context_soft <name> <pct>` (once per crew per soft crossing, tracked in `mem`), `hung <name>` (`working`, transcript size unchanged for `hung_s`), `settled <name> <status>` (`idle`/`done`/`blocked` with `bg_shells == 0`; `blocked` always) — Review Focus 2, `error <source>` (a source failing `monitor.error_polls` polls in a row, once per streak — follow-up fix). `mem` is updated on a copy and committed only when the whole poll returns; one failing source never blocks the others.
  - `main(argv)` — `monitor.py [--crew NAME ...] [--interval S] [--heartbeat S]` (without `--crew` it follows `journal.crews()` on every poll): loops `poll` every `interval_s`; on the first non-empty result prints `EVENT <line>` for each line and exits 0; after `heartbeat_s` without events prints `EVENT heartbeat` and exits 0; `mem` persists in `$PRORAB_LOG_DIR/monitor-state.json`, so a condition that persists across restarts fires once; appends one log line per poll to `~/projects/prorab/logs/monitor.log`; each line carries the limit reading (`ts`, `five_hour`, `seven_day`, `source`, `resets_at`) so `scheduler.burn_rate` and the morning report can read it back (`readings(since: float | None = None) -> list[dict]`).
  - While `journal.override()` is set: no `limit` and no `budget` events; readings are still logged (S §4) — Review Focus 7.
  - G (Task 8b.8): one `agent list` and one `kv list` per poll, screens read only on change, interval backoff; `hung` by two signs; new events `consent`, `limit_screen`, `provider_error`, `hung_unclear`.
  - R (Task 8c.7): the `budget` event in dollars (`usd`, `estimate:<id>`, `research:<key>`, `week_pp`); the spend and the rate computed by reading files in-process and logged.

### Task 8: Limits sensor

**Files:**
- Create: `prorab/scripts/sensor.py`, `prorab/scripts/statusline_tap.sh`, `prorab/tests/test_sensor.py`

L: one file per session instead of one shared file (idle sessions rewrite theirs with a frozen percent).
Stop-point-8 decision (the foreman, follow-up commit): the tap is POSIX `sh` (`#!/bin/sh`), no python, no jq — it runs in every session every minute and after every message, and every process start is inspected by the machine's security agents; overhead ≤ 10 ms over the original command (20 runs, measured in a sandbox, numbers in the report). `session_id` via sh parameter expansion, validated with `case`; invalid → write nothing but still run the original. Atomic write: `printf` to a temp file in the same directory, then `mv`. The tap only writes; pruning files older than `limits.sensor_retention_s` moves to the reader (`limits`). `read_sensor` groups `resets_at` with a 10-minute tolerance instead of exact equality.
Follow-up commits (the foreman's rulings): `statusLine.command` = `. <quoted tap path>` — the tap is sourced into the shell Claude Code already starts, so it adds no process of its own except one `mv`; a single-line original whose first word is a file is exec'd with stdin from the written file, anything else runs in a subshell; the command's exit code is the original's, tap failures exit 0; the stdin the original gets is byte-identical on every path.

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `statusline_tap.sh` — reads all of stdin; writes it atomically (temp file + `mv`) to `$PRORAB_CACHE/usage/<session_id>.json` (`session_id` from the JSON; `PRORAB_CACHE` default `~/.cache/prorab`); if `$PRORAB_CACHE/statusline.orig` (the original command) exists, runs it with the same stdin and passes its stdout through; never prints anything of its own.
  - `install(settings: str, cache: str, tap: str) -> dict` — backs up `settings` to `settings.prorab-backup-<YYYYmmddHHMMSS>` (same mode); saves the original `statusLine.command` (if any) to `cache/statusline.orig` and the original `statusLine` object to `cache/statusline.state.json`; sets `statusLine.command` to the sourced tap, preserving `type`, `padding`, `refreshInterval`; idempotent (a second install changes nothing).
  - `uninstall(settings: str, cache: str) -> dict` — restores the original `statusLine` exactly, or removes `statusLine` if there was none; removes `cache/usage/`.
  - `status(cache: str, max_age_s: int) -> dict` — `{"installed", "fresh", "age_s", "sessions"}` (`fresh` = at least one fresh session file; `age_s` of the newest; `sessions` = number of fresh files).
  - CLI: `sensor.py install|uninstall|status` with real paths; `install` prints what it will change and requires `--yes` (the foreman passes it only after the customer agreed).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing tests** (temp HOME/settings/cache): `test_install_preserves_fields_and_backs_up`, `test_install_idempotent`, `test_uninstall_restores_exactly` (byte-identical `statusLine` object), `test_tap_passes_output_through` (orig = `printf X`; tap stdout `X`; `usage/<session_id>.json` equals stdin), `test_tap_per_session_files_and_cleanup` (two session ids → two files; a file older than the retention is deleted by the reader), `test_uninstall_removes_usage_dir`, `test_tap_without_orig_prints_nothing`, `test_cli_install_requires_yes`; the follow-up commits add the sourced-tap, exec, exit-code and exact-stdin tests, under `sh` and `zsh`.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Run → FAIL. **Step 3:** Implement. **Step 4:** Run → PASS.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** `feat(sensor): optional statusline tap for exact Claude Max limits`.

## Remaining tasks

### Task 8a: English identifiers (E)

One commit, right after the two docs commits (spec and plan) and before Task 8b. Every Russian-derived identifier takes its glossary term; only `prorab` and names derived from it stay. Behaviour is unchanged and the full suite passes. Cyrillic strings, docstrings and comments stay for Task 8d — but every Latin-letter occurrence of a banned stem goes now, comments and English docstrings included, because the guard of this task scans whole files. Nothing real depends on the old names yet: the real journal is created only in Task 13, and the sensor's `~/.cache/prorab/usage/` is already English. Names that exist only in the plan (Tasks 8b–14) are written with the new names directly.

**Files:**
- Rename (`git mv`): `prorab/scripts/{cr}.py` → `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/tests/test_{cr}.py` → `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`
- Modify: every file under `prorab/scripts/` and `prorab/tests/` that holds an old name, `prorab/references/defaults.json`, `prorab/references/acts.md` (the headings the scripts read and the drop marker only)
- Create: `prorab/tests/test_language.py`

**The banned words.** The guard bans eight transliterated stems and one abbreviation. This plan may not contain them literally (the guard scans `docs/`), so they are given here as the string pieces the test concatenates, and `test_language.py` builds its list exactly this way — so the test file does not trip its own guard either:

```python
BANNED_STEMS = ("nar" + "yad", "dop" + "usk", "sme" + "ta", "bri" + "gad",
                "obe" + "kt", "pri" + "emk", "pere" + "smen", "zakaz" + "chik")
```

In order: the Russian words for work order, clearance, estimate, crew, site, acceptance, handover and customer. A stem matches case-insensitively anywhere in a line, inside identifiers and longer words too (so the old English word for crew built on the fourth stem is banned as well). The abbreviation of "object", `"o" + "bj"`, is banned only as a whole identifier: case-insensitive and not flanked by ASCII letters — `(?<![A-Za-z])` + `"o" + "bj"` + `(?![A-Za-z])` — so `object`, `objective` and `mock.patch.object` pass, while the bare abbreviation, the abbreviation before `_path` or a digit, and its upper-case `_DIR` form hit (`_` and digits are not letters).

**The old → new table.** The "old" column uses placeholders by design, because the old spellings hold the banned words: `{wo}` = `"nar" + "yad"` (`{WO}` in upper-case constants); `{cr}` = `"bri" + "gad" + "e"`, the old English word for crew (`{Cr}` capitalised, `{cr}s` plural); `{est}` = `"sme" + "ta"`; `{cl}` = `"dop" + "usk"`; `{ob}` = `"o" + "bj"` (`{OB}` in upper case). Cyrillic names are given by meaning. The implementer maps the old names by meaning, and the guard's own word list finds every remaining occurrence, including any the table misses.

### Task 8b: Amendment G for the finished tasks

G (customer-approved 2026-10-21): patterns adopted from Gas City, applied to the finished Tasks 1–7, with the foreman's answers 1–3 folded in. Ideas only: no Gas City code or fixture is copied; every fixture below is synthetic and neutral. Each sub-task is TDD (failing tests first) and **one commit**; the full suite passes after every commit. Every string these sub-tasks add is English. Run them in this order — later sub-tasks consume earlier interfaces, and `limits` reuses `herdr_cli.bottom_block`, so Task 4's sub-task comes before Task 3's:

8b.1 (Task 1) → 8b.2 (Task 2) → 8b.3 (Task 4) → 8b.4 (Task 3) → 8b.5 (Task 5) → 8b.6 (Task 6) → 8b.7 (Task 6b) → 8b.8 (Task 7).

Kept as they are: the monitor's memory persists across restarts, and every event — old and new — fires once per change or episode; `answer()` never answers a consent prompt (`needs_trust` / `needs_consent`); an unknown usage or limit value is `None`, never 0; the status-line tap (POSIX sh, sourced) is not touched.

| Pattern | Sub-tasks | Later tasks |
|---|---|---|
| P1 reliable send, multi-line by file | 8b.1, 8b.3, 8b.6 | 13 |
