#### 8b.3 — Task 4: herdr wrapper (P1, P7, P12; answer 3)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/herdr_cli.py`, `prorab/tests/test_herdr_cli.py`; Create `prorab/tests/fixtures/consent/` — `trust.txt`, `mcp.txt`, `claude_md_imports.txt`, `bypass_warning.txt`, `api_key.txt`, `tool_permission.txt`, `marker_in_scrollback.txt` — and `prorab/tests/fixtures/composer/` — `ours.txt` (the composer line holds the start of the sent line), `empty.txt` (an empty composer), `suggestion.txt` (the composer shows a prompt suggestion that is not our text: in plain screen text a grey suggestion looks like typed text), `legacy_marker.txt` (the same layout with the older `>` marker, R45) — synthetic screens in Claude's layout, written for this repo

**Interfaces:**
- `bottom_block(visible: str, n: int) -> str` — the last `n` non-empty lines of a screen. Every screen marker of the amendment is searched only there: old output above may contain the same words.
- `CONSENT_KINDS` — ordered `((kind, (markers, ...)), ...)`, case-insensitive substrings, first match wins: `api_key` ("use this api key"); `trust` ("trust this folder", "quick safety check", "do you trust the files"); `bypass_warning` ("bypass permissions mode"); `claude_md_imports` ("claude.md file imports", "external claude.md"); `mcp` ("mcp server"); `tool_permission` ("esc to cancel · tab to amend", "do you want to proceed", "do you want to make this edit", "do you want to create", "do you want to allow", "don't ask again"). Markers stay broad: a false positive only stops the crew, a miss could let a consent prompt be answered as a question. Replaces `crew.CONSENT_MARKERS` (still one constant, now here). `CONSENT_NAMES` — English names for gates and reports: "folder trust", "the project's MCP servers", "external CLAUDE.md imports", "bypass-mode warning", "custom API key", "tool permission".
- `consent_dialog(visible: str, tail_lines: int | None = None) -> str | None` — the kind found in `bottom_block(visible, tail_lines or screen.tail_lines)`, else `None`.
- `choice_state(visible)` / `choice_dialog(visible)` — `None` whenever `consent_dialog(visible)` is not `None`: a permission prompt is never a choice question.
- `composer_text(visible: str, tail_lines: int | None = None) -> str | None` — Claude Code's input line in the bottom block (R45, verified on a live Claude Code pane): the last line that starts with the prompt marker `❯` (U+276F), followed by a space or the end of the line, and sits directly between two horizontal-rule lines of `─` (U+2500); `>` is accepted as a fallback for older versions under the same rule; a choice-dialog line such as `❯ 1. Yes` is never a composer. Returns the text after the marker with trailing spaces stripped (`""` for an empty composer, the bare `❯`), `None` when no such line is visible. Task 13 confirms the marker on a real crew pane.
- `send(name: str, text: str, timeout_ms: int | None = None) -> str` — `agent prompt NAME TEXT --wait --until working --until done --until blocked --timeout MS` (default `send.timeout_ms`; no `--until idle`: the agent is idle before the prompt, so a swallowed Enter would read as delivery; R46: herdr's `--wait` gate already requires an observed `working` or `blocked` from a non-working state, so `done` here follows a real turn). The status is read from the JSON herdr prints; an output with no recognisable status falls back to `agent get` (R46; Task 13 checks where the field is). Returns `"accepted"` (exit 0 with status `working` or `done`; any other status → `"unconfirmed"`), `"blocked"` (status `blocked`, or error `agent_blocked`), `"stalled"` (`agent_prompt_stalled`), `"unconfirmed"` (`timeout` and the agent is not `working` afterwards: nothing is pressed, nothing re-sent — previously this re-raised); `timeout` while `working` stays `"accepted"`; anything else re-raises. Never sends the text twice. (Multi-line text is refused in 8b.6, together with the file delivery that replaces it.)
- `recover_stalled(name: str, text: str, timeout_ms: int | None = None) -> str` — answer 3: `agent get NAME`; only if `agent_status` is `idle` or `done` (herdr shows `done`, not `idle`, after a Claude turn, so the two are treated alike) **and** `composer_text(agent_visible(name))` is non-empty and equals `text` in its first `min(send.composer_match_chars, len(text))` characters: exactly one `agent send-keys NAME enter`, then `agent wait NAME --until working --until blocked --timeout MS` (R46: no `--until done` — a standalone `agent wait` matches the current state, and the pane was already `idle` or `done`, so `done` would not prove the Enter was taken) → `working` gives `"accepted"`, `blocked` gives `"blocked"`, anything else or a timeout gives `"unconfirmed"`, and nothing more is pressed; a wait output with no recognisable status falls back to `agent get`. Any other status before the Enter, an empty composer (a grey prompt suggestion there could be submitted), a composer showing other text, or no composer line → `"stalled"` with no keys (an Enter could answer a dialog or submit a suggestion).
- `wait_ready(name: str, timeout_ms: int | None = None) -> str` — `agent wait NAME --until idle --until done --timeout MS` before the first prompt (a finished turn shows `done`, not `idle`); returns the reached status or `"timeout"`.
- `valid_name(name: str) -> bool` — herdr's rule `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$`.
- `agent_by_pane(pane_id: str, agents_list: list[dict] | None = None) -> dict | None` — pane numbers are reused and names can be reset, so after a handover a crew is found by its pane and its name is only compared.

Yes` between two rules → `None`); `test_recover_stalled_idle_or_done_with_our_text_one_enter` (status `idle`, then `done`: each exactly one `send-keys … enter`, no further `prompt`, then `agent wait … --until working --until blocked`) — Review Focus 4; R46: `test_recover_stalled_wait_outcomes` (timeout → `"unconfirmed"`, `blocked` → `"blocked"`), `test_recover_stalled_swallowed_enter_is_never_accepted` (the wait answers `done` → `"unconfirmed"`), `test_send_without_status_falls_back_to_agent_get`, `test_recover_stalled_without_status_falls_back_to_agent_get`; `test_recover_stalled_empty_composer_presses_nothing` (status `done`, `empty.txt` → `"stalled"`, no `send-keys`) — answer 3; `test_recover_stalled_suggestion_presses_nothing` (`suggestion.txt` → `"stalled"`, no keys); `test_recover_stalled_other_status_presses_nothing` (`working`, `blocked`); `test_wait_ready_until_idle_or_done`; `test_consent_kinds_from_fixtures` (each fixture → its kind); `test_consent_only_in_bottom_block` (`marker_in_scrollback.txt` → `None`) — Review Focus 10; `test_bypass_status_line_is_not_consent` (`bypass permissions on · 1 shell` → `None`); `test_tool_permission_is_not_a_choice_dialog` — Review Focus 3; `test_valid_name` (`demo-app-1` ok; `Demo-1`, `1demo`, 33 characters → False); `test_agent_by_pane`.

#### 8b.4 — Task 3: limits (P3)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/limits.py`, `prorab/tests/test_limits.py`; Create `prorab/tests/fixtures/limit_screen.txt`, `limit_choice.txt` (a choice dialog "1. Stop and wait for limit to reset" / "2. Upgrade your plan" with "Upgrade" highlighted), `limit_in_scrollback.txt`, `provider_error.txt` — synthetic placeholders until Task 13 (answer 5)

**Interfaces:**
- `detect_messages(text, patterns, tail_lines: int | None = None) -> bool` — with `tail_lines`, only `herdr_cli.bottom_block(text, tail_lines)`; `get_limits` passes `screen.tail_lines`, so an old limit message in the scrollback no longer sets `reached`.
- `parse_resets(text: str, now: float) -> int | None` — "resets 7pm", "resets 7:30pm", "resets 19:00", optionally followed by a zone in parentheses — `(UTC)`, `(GMT)`, `(Region/City)` — resolved with stdlib `zoneinfo`; an unresolvable zone gives an unknown reset (`None`), never local time (R47); without a zone, local time; the first such moment after `now`, unix seconds; no match → `None`. Supersedes "not parsed until Task 13" for this form; Task 13 confirms the wording.
- `limit_screen(text: str, d: dict, now: float | None = None) -> dict | None` — on the bottom block only: `{"kind": "limit_choice", "options": [...], "highlighted": i, "wait_option": j}` when a choice dialog (`herdr_cli.choice_state`) has an option whose label starts with `limits.limit_choice.wait`; `{"kind": "limit"}` when a `limits.limit_screen` pattern is present; both carry `"resets_at": parse_resets(<bottom block>, now)`; else `None`. `wait_option` is never an option that starts with `limits.limit_choice.upgrade`.
- `provider_error(text: str, d: dict) -> str | None` — the first `limits.fatal_patterns` entry found in the bottom block.
- `get_limits(pane_texts, d, now=None)` — `pane_texts` may be a list or a zero-argument callable; the callable is called only when the sensor gives nothing (the monitor's process budget, 8b.8). The `messages` source now carries `five_hour_resets_at` from `parse_resets` of the matching pane.

#### 8b.5 — Task 5: journal (P5, P8, P10, P11, P13; answer 1)

Answer 1 assigns P5 (iteration counters), P10 (one push per gate) and P11 (the site passport) to the journal; the issue records (P13) and the kv snapshot (P8) are journal helpers too, so they land here.

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/journal.py`, `prorab/tests/test_journal.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `bump_iterations(stage: str, kind: str) -> int`, `iterations(stage: str, kind: str) -> int` — kv `iterations.<stage>.<kind>`; `kind` is a key of `cycles` (`approval`, `review`, `report_fix`), anything else → `ValueError`. `cycle_exceeded(stage: str, kind: str, d: dict | None = None) -> bool` — `iterations(...) > cycles.<kind>`: the 7th approval request, the 11th review round or the 4th report fix means a gate.
- `mark_notified(key: str) -> bool` — `True` and kv `notified.<key>` = unix time the first time, `False` afterwards; `key` is a gate id or an incident key (e.g. `api_key.<crew>`). At most one push per gate, across monitor restarts and foreman handovers. `notified() -> dict[str, str]` for the morning report.
- `passport(site: str) -> dict | None` (`{"id", "text"}`), `set_passport(site: str, text: str) -> str` — one bead per site, labels `prorab:passport,site:<site>`; `set_passport` creates it or replaces its description and returns its id. The passport lives in the journal (ignored by git), never in the site or the skill.
- `issue_set(work_order: str, state: str, **fields) -> None`, `issue(work_order: str) -> dict | None`, `issues(state: str | None = None) -> dict[str, dict]` — kv `issue.<work_order>` = JSON `{"state": "issuing" | "issued" | "not_issued", "name", "pane", "session_id", "ts", ...}`; `issue_set` merges `fields` into the existing record (8c.5 adds `worktree`); any other state → `ValueError`.
- Keyword `kv: dict | None = None` (a `kv_all()` snapshot) on `budget`, `shift_spent`, `over_budget`, `override` and `crews`: with a snapshot they start no `bd` process for kv; `override` still lists the goal's work orders, but only while the override is set.
- CLI: `journal.py iterations STAGE KIND [--bump]` → `{"n", "limit", "exceeded"}`; `journal.py notify KEY` → `{"push": true | false}`; `journal.py passport SITE [--set-file F]`; `journal.py issues [--state S]`.

#### 8b.6 — Task 6: crew (P1, P3, P4, P6, P7, P9, P12, P13; answers 2 and 3)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/scripts/herdr_cli.py` (only: `send` raises `ValueError` on text with a newline), `prorab/references/acts.md` (new sections, same layout: scripts read the first fenced block under a heading), `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`, `prorab/tests/test_herdr_cli.py` (`test_send_refuses_multiline`)

**Interfaces:**
- `name_for(site: str, n: int) -> str` — the basename of `site` lowercased; every character outside `[a-z0-9_-]` → `-`; repeats collapsed; a leading non-letter gets the prefix `c-`; the base truncated so that `<base>-<n>` has at most 32 characters; the result always passes `herdr_cli.valid_name`. CLI `crew.py name_for SITE N`.
- `message_file(work_order: str | None, text: str, name: str | None = None) -> str` — writes into `send.files_dir` (`~/.cache/prorab/work-orders/`, answer 2), directory 0700, file 0600, temp file + `os.replace`; returns the path. With `name` → `<name>.md` (the work order itself is `<work_order>.md`, replaced in place); else `<work_order>-<k>.md` (k = next free number), or `msg-<unix time>.md` without a work order. The file lives in the prorab cache, never in the site or the skill.
- `deliver(name: str, text: str, work_order: str | None = None) -> str` — the only way a message reaches a crew (`send_work_order`, `request_handover`, `pause`, `resume`, `answer` without a dialog, restarts; `/exit` stays a plain `send`). A single line → `herdr_cli.send`; multi-line → `message_file` + the one line of `## Message by file`. `"stalled"` → `herdr_cli.recover_stalled(name, <the line sent>)` — never a second `prompt`, and Enter only when the composer holds the start of that line (answer 3); `"blocked"` → `herdr_cli.consent_dialog(agent_visible)` → `"consent:<kind>"` when it is one. Returns `"accepted"` | `"blocked"` | `"stalled"` | `"unconfirmed"` | `"consent:<kind>"`.
- `send_work_order(name: str, text: str, work_order: str | None = None) -> str` — the work order text (with the site passport the caller appended) goes to `message_file(work_order, text, name=work_order)` and the crew gets the one line of `## Message by file` through `deliver` (work order from kv `crew.<name>.work_order` when `None`); on `"accepted"` → `journal.issue_set(work_order, "issued", ...)`. The 5-second retry of the whole text is removed.
- `start(site_path, work_order, name, caller_pane)` — new order: `herdr_cli.valid_name(name)` (else `{"status": "bad_name"}`; the foreman takes `name_for`); `journal.issue(work_order)` in state `issuing` or `issued` whose pane still holds an agent → `{"status": "already_issued", "name", "pane_id"}`; the existing parallel and file-overlap checks; split the pane; `session_id = str(uuid.uuid4())`; `journal.issue_set(work_order, "issuing", name=, pane=, session_id=)` **before** `agent start NAME --kind claude --pane P -- --session-id <uuid> --add-dir <send.files_dir> -w prorab-<work_order>` (answer 2: on every start, bypass mode or not, so reading the work-order file needs no permission outside the worktree). On `agent_not_ready` → `herdr_cli.consent_dialog(pane_visible(P))`: `trust` → `{"status": "needs_trust", "pane_id"}`, any other kind → `{"status": "needs_consent", "kind", "pane_id"}`, none → `not_ready`; never keys. On success also kv `crew.<name>.session` = the uuid and `crew.<name>.transcript` = `transcript.transcript_path(<worktree>, uuid)`; the result's `session_id` is the assigned uuid.
- `state(name: str, a: dict | None = None, *, read_screen: bool = True, screen: str | None = None, kv: dict | None = None) -> dict` — finds the agent by its pane (kv `crew.<name>.pane`, `herdr_cli.agent_by_pane`), the name only as a fallback; adds `name_ok`. With `read_screen` one `agent_visible` read gives `bg_shells`, `consent` (`consent_dialog`), `limit_screen` (`limits.limit_screen`), `provider_error` (`limits.provider_error`); `screen` is a screen text the caller has already read, used instead of a new read (R51; `""` is a read screen with no markers, not an unread one); without either those keys are `None` (unknown, not 0). Transcript from kv `crew.<name>.transcript` first.
- `wait_limit(name: str) -> dict` — `limits.limit_screen(agent_visible(name))`: `limit_choice` → move from `highlighted` to `wait_option` (`up`/`down`) and `enter` — the "Upgrade" option is never selected; `limit` → no keys; a choice without a "Stop and wait" option → `{"status": "unknown_limit_dialog"}`, nothing pressed (the foreman opens a gate). Records act `answer` "crew waits for the limit reset until HH:MM", kv `crew.<name>.limit_until` = `resets_at`; never calls `journal.bump_restarts`. Returns `{"status": "waiting", "resets_at"}` or `{"status": "no_limit_screen"}`.
- `resume(name: str) -> str` — `deliver` of the line under `## Continue` (after a limit reset or a `--resume`).
- `answer(name, choice=None, text=None) -> str` — a consent prompt → `"consent:<kind>"`, nothing sent; any limit screen → `"limit_screen"`, nothing sent (only `wait_limit` acts there); multi-line `text` without a dialog → `deliver`.
- `restart(name: str, work_order: str, work_order_text: str, reason: str, caller_pane: str | None = None) -> dict` — `reason` ∈ `gone`, `hung`, `limit_died`. For `gone` / `hung`: when one more restart would exceed `monitor.max_restarts` → `journal.wait_customer(work_order, ...)`, `{"status": "waits_customer"}`, nothing started. `limit_died` (the session ended while waiting for a reset) never counts. The restart point is a successful `agent start` (R48, R49): from then on kv `crew.<name>.session` names the session that runs (the new uuid; the old one on `--resume`) and `journal.bump_restarts(work_order)` counts the restart, whatever the delivery result (a delivery that is not accepted → `{"status": "prompt_not_accepted", ...}`, the session stays recorded); a failure before `agent start` (the pane, the `cd`, a herdr error, an unknown worktree) changes nothing, is not counted and returns `{"status": "restart_failed", "reason"}`. In the crew's pane (a new split of `caller_pane` when the pane is gone), after `cd <worktree>` as in `complete_handover`: if `transcript.context_status` of the old session is not `soft` and kv `crew.<name>.session` is set → `agent start NAME --kind claude --pane P -- --resume <uuid> --add-dir <send.files_dir>`, then `resume(name)` → `{"status": "resumed"}`; if the context is soft, or the bottom block shows `screen.resume_failed` → `-- --session-id <new uuid> --add-dir <send.files_dir>` and `deliver` of the work-order text plus all `handover` acts → `{"status": "restarted"}`. Consent screens map as in `start`.
- `complete_handover(name, work_order, work_order_text, timeout_s=60, poll_s=1, base="main") -> dict` — a second handover of the same work order within `handover.min_interval_s` of the last one (kv `handover.<work_order>.last`) → `journal.wait_customer(work_order, "handover loop")`, `{"status": "handover_loop"}`, no `/exit`. The act gets a block `## Facts at handover`: `git status --short` and `git log --oneline <base>..HEAD` in the worktree, the status of the work order's open stage from `bd show` (read only). `/exit` is sent only after `journal.add_act` returned; if it raised → `{"status": "act_not_saved"}`, no `/exit`. The new session gets `-- --session-id <new uuid> --add-dir <send.files_dir>` (kv updated) and the work-order text plus all acts by `deliver` (one line naming a file). Never calls `journal.bump_restarts`.
- `check_report(text: str, criteria: list[str], files: list[str], worktree: str, base: str = "main") -> dict` — read-only, returns `{"ok", "problems": [str], "warnings": [str], "outcome", "sha"}` (problems and warnings in English). Constants: `REPORT_SECTIONS` = ("Summary", "Behaviour", "Changed files", "Verification", "Residual risks"), `CRITERION_STATUSES` = ("done", "not applicable", "deferred", "blocked"), `OUTCOMES` = ("delivered", "no changes", "blocked", "abandoned"). Format: a section is a heading line (`#`…`####`) with that name; a criterion is a table row `| C<n> | <status> | <reason> |`; `Result: <outcome>`; `Commit: <7–40 hex>`; changed files are the list items under "Changed files"; every list item under "Verification" holds a command in backticks and `SKIP: <n>`. Problems: a missing section; a C-id of `criteria` missing; a status outside the list; a status other than "done" without a reason; no or unknown outcome; for "delivered": no SHA, the SHA not an ancestor of `HEAD` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`) or not among the commits of `<base>..HEAD` (R48), no commit in `<base>..HEAD`, an empty `git diff <base>...HEAD`, any other git failure (refusal, never a pass); an empty item under "Changed files" (a rejection, never a crash — R48); the listed files differ from `git diff --name-only <base>...HEAD` or fall outside `files` (`fnmatch`; an entry ending in `/` covers its subtree; an empty `files` skips this check); a check command without `SKIP: <n>`; "delivered" without any check command. Warnings: `SKIP: <n>` with n > 0 (the foreman judges the skipped tests and says so in the act). `criteria_ids(work_order_text: str) -> list[str]` — the `C<n>` ids at line starts, in order.
- `report_fix(name: str, work_order: str, stage: str, problems: list[str]) -> dict` — `journal.bump_iterations(stage, "report_fix")`; `cycle_exceeded` → `journal.wait_customer(stage, ...)`, `{"status": "waits_customer"}`; else `message_file(work_order, <problems>)` + the line of `## Fix the report` → `{"status": "sent", "attempt": n}`. CLI `crew.py check_report NAME WORK_ORDER [--base main]` (report = `summary(NAME)`, criteria from the work order's description, files = `journal.work_order_files`, worktree from kv) and `crew.py report_fix NAME WORK_ORDER STAGE --problems-file F`.
- `ask_predecessor(work_order: str, session_id: str, cwd: str, question: str) -> str` — a manual tool only (never called by the monitor or other scripts): `journal.spend(work_order, runs=1)` first, then `claude --resume <session_id> --fork-session -p <question>` in `cwd` with `crew.ask_timeout_s`; returns stdout; the predecessor's session is not changed. CLI `crew.py ask_predecessor WORK_ORDER SESSION CWD QUESTION`.
- `stop(name, work_order)` — also clears kv `crew.<name>.session`, `.transcript`, `.limit_until`.
- `acts.md`, new sections (English): `## Message by file` — "Read the file {path} and do what it says."; `## Continue` — "Continue from where you stopped."; `## Fix the report` — "The stage report failed the check; the mismatches are listed in the file {path}. Fix the report or the work and send the report again." (`{path}` filled by `str.format`).

#### 8b.8 — Task 7: monitor (P3, P7, P8)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/monitor.py`, `prorab/tests/test_monitor.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `poll(crews, d, mem, now, *, deps=None)` — same signature; `deps` gains `agents` (`herdr_cli.agents`), `kv_all` (`journal.kv_all`), `visible` (pane id → screen text), `slot_holders` (`slots.holders`), `newest_age` (`(worktree, now, max_files) -> float | None`: age of the newest file, skipping `.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`; `None` when unreadable or more than `max_files` entries); `state` is called as `state(name, a, read_screen=False, screen=<the text read this poll, or None>, kv=...)` (R51).
- **Process budget.** Per poll exactly one `agents()` and one `kv_all()`; `kv_get`, `over_budget`, `override`, `crews` and the foreman's own context use them (8b.5 snapshot). A crew's screen is read only when its herdr status differs from the last poll, when it was `idle`/`done` with `bg_shells > 0` at the last read, or when its context comes only from the status line. Limits go through `scheduler.gather_limits` with a callable that is invoked only without a fresh sensor; it returns the screens already read this poll and, while fewer than `monitor.limit_panes` of them belong to `working` crews, reads further `working` crews' screens up to that number. A window no pane shows stays `None`, never 0.
- **New events**, each once per episode (state in `mem`, persisted like the rest): `consent <name> <kind>` (instead of `settled <name> blocked` for that screen); `provider_error <name> <reason>` (instead of `settled` for that crew's episode, R50: a final provider error is a gate, not a finished stage); `limit_screen <name> <resets_at or ->` (instead of `settled`; suppresses `hung` for that crew); `hung_unclear <name> <reason>` (`no_transcript`, `no_worktree`, `scan_capped` — reported, never a restart). Priority: `gone`, `consent`, `provider_error`, `limit_screen`, `limit`, `blind`, `blind_week`, `budget`, `prorab_context`, `context_hard`, `context_soft`, `hung`, `hung_unclear`, `settled`, `error`. (Task 14's candidate event `compacted` goes right after `prorab_context`, before `context_hard`.)
- **`hung <name>`** only when both signs hold: the transcript size unchanged for `hung_s` **and** `newest_age(worktree)` ≥ `hung_s` (the worktree is scanned only after the first sign holds); never while a `slot_holders()` record has its `cwd` inside the crew's worktree. When a sign cannot be read → `hung_unclear` instead, never `hung`.
- **Backoff.** `main` sleeps `mem["interval"]`: it starts at `monitor.interval_s`; after a poll with no status change, no event and no `working` crew it is multiplied by `monitor.backoff_factor` up to `monitor.interval_max_s`; any status change, event or `working` crew resets it. `--interval` fixes it; the heartbeat (`heartbeat_s`) is unchanged.

### Task 8c: Amendment R for the finished tasks

R (customer decision 2026-10-21): estimates, spend and the shift budget are in US dollars at API prices; the rate converts dollars into pp of the window and of the week; counts stay only as machine protection; the foreman's own research and one-off subagents run under dollar estimates; the crew handover comes at about 40 % of the context; overspend means a stop and a gate, as before. The reference calculation the price table and the formula come from is read-only and outside this repo; nothing of it is copied. Each sub-task is TDD and **one commit**; the full suite passes after every commit; every new string is English. Order:

8c.1 (Task 1) → 8c.2 (Task 2) → 8c.3 (Task 3) → 8c.4 (Task 5) → 8c.5 (Task 6) → 8c.6 (Task 6b) → 8c.7 (Task 7).

The per-transcript cost lives in Task 2's module (8c.2): the spec puts all reading of transcripts there ("compute the spend in dollars from transcripts — per work order, the foreman's own, the machine's"), and the rate, the journal, the plan and the monitor all consume it. Tasks 6 and 6b get sub-tasks too, because the spec changes acceptance (heavy runs and spend against the estimate) and issuing (the estimate in pp by the rate, checked before issuing). All transcript tests use a temp `HOME` with synthetic `~/.claude/projects/` trees — never the real one.

**Keys** (values verbatim):
- `context.soft_pct`: 60 → **40** (R40: every turn of a long session re-reads its whole context); `context.soft_max_tokens` 500000 and `context.hard_pct` 80 unchanged.
- `budget = {"usd": 350, "week_pp": 10}` — the shift's spend at API prices and the rise of the week limit during the shift; `sessions` and `runs` leave it.
- `machine = {"parallel_ceiling": 6, "heavy_slots": 2, "heavy_runs_per_stage": 20}` — machine protection, counts not budget: the two counts move here from `budget`, plus the default heavy-run limit per stage (a work order may set its own, 8c.4).
- `cost = {"prices": {"opus": {"input": 4.0, "output": 20.0, "cache_read": 0.2}, "sonnet": {"input": 2.0, "output": 10.0, "cache_read": 0.2}, "haiku": {"input": 0.1, "output": 0.5, "cache_read": 0.01}, "fable": {"input": 10.0, "output": 50.0, "cache_read": 0.25}}, "cache_write_5m_factor": 1.25, "cache_write_1h_factor": 2.0}` — dollars per million tokens; the family is the first key (in table order) that is a substring of `message.model`; updated here by hand, never in code.
- `rate = {"default_window_usd_per_pp": 5.0, "default_week_usd_per_pp": null, "min_rise_pp": 1, "window_horizon_s": 18000, "every_s": 600}` — the window rate until the first estimate; no week default (unknown, never guessed); the minimum rise of an interval; the 5-hour horizon for the window; re-estimate at most every 10 minutes. The minimum span of an interval stays `schedule.burn_min_span_s` (600), shared with the window's consumption speed (`scheduler.burn_rate`), which is not the dollar rate.
- `monitor.spend_every_s = 300` — the monitor recomputes the spend at most this often (8c.7).
- `polish_cmd` removed (R39).

**Readers:**
- `journal.machine(kv: dict | None = None) -> dict` — `defaults["machine"]` overlaid with kv `machine.<key>` (int); CLI `journal.py machine [--set k=v ...]`. The customer replaces `parallel_ceiling` here.

- `scheduler.allowed_parallel(..., d)` — the ceiling from `d["machine"]["parallel_ceiling"]`; `build_plan` passes `dict(d, machine=j.machine())`, so a kv ceiling counts in the plan too.
- `slots.slot_count` — falls back to `machine.heavy_slots`.
- `crew.start` — the ceiling from `journal.machine()["parallel_ceiling"]`.

#### 8c.2 — Task 2: spend in dollars from transcripts

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/transcript.py`, `prorab/tests/test_transcript.py`; Create `prorab/tests/fixtures/cost/` — `main.jsonl` (two messages, one of them streamed as two lines with the same `message.id`, output 10 then 50), `fork.jsonl` (a forked session repeating that id), `subagent.jsonl`, `no_split.jsonl` (usage with `cache_creation_input_tokens` and no `cache_creation` object), `sidechain.jsonl` (a sidechain line with usage), `unknown_model.jsonl` — synthetic; tests copy them into a temp `HOME` under `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/…`, with `cwd` fields pointing into temp paths

**Interfaces:**
- `price_for(model: str | None, d: dict) -> dict` — `{"input", "output", "cache_read"}`: the first `cost.prices` family whose name is a substring of `model`; otherwise the column-wise maximum over all families (never cheaper than any known model; today exactly the fable row).
- `message_cost(usage: dict, model: str | None, d: dict) -> float` — the spec's formula, dollars: (`input_tokens` × input + `cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` × `cache_write_5m_factor` × input + `cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` × `cache_write_1h_factor` × input + `cache_read_input_tokens` × cache_read + `output_tokens` × output) / 1 000 000; when the usage has neither split field, the whole `cache_creation_input_tokens` is priced at the 1-hour factor (the dearer rate); missing counts are 0.
- `session_files(project_dir: str) -> list[str]` — the `*.jsonl` files directly in it plus `*/subagents/*.jsonl`, sorted.
- `session_cost_files(transcript_path: str) -> list[str]` — a session's transcript plus its `<dir>/<session id>/subagents/*.jsonl`.
- `worktree_files(worktree: str, home: str | None = None) -> list[str]` — the `session_files` of every project directory under `<home>/.claude/projects/` whose name equals `project_slug(worktree)` or starts with it followed by `-` (the paths below the worktree: `claude -p` runs started in a subdirectory), kept only when the `cwd` of the first entry that has one is the worktree or a path below it (`os.path.realpath`; the slug maps `/` and `-` alike, so `/x/wt-other` must not count for `/x/wt`).
- `machine_files(since: float | None = None, home: str | None = None) -> list[str]` — every `session_files` under `<home>/.claude/projects/`; files whose mtime is older than `since` are skipped unopened.
- `spend_series(paths: list[str], d: dict, since: float | None = None, until: float | None = None, seen: dict | None = None) -> list[tuple[float, float]]` — `(timestamp, usd)` per assistant message, in time order. Every line whose `type` is `assistant` and that has `message.usage` counts — sidechain lines included (the cost sums all work, while `last_usage` still reads only the main chain). Every `message.id` counts once across all `paths`: of its copies the one with the largest `output_tokens` is kept (streamed lines and forked or resumed sessions repeat an id); a line without an id counts on its own. The line's `timestamp` (ISO 8601) must fall in `[since, until)`; unparsable lines are skipped. `seen`, shared between calls, makes an id counted by an earlier call count no more.
- `cost(paths, d, since=None, until=None, seen=None) -> dict` — `{"usd": float, "messages": int, "tokens": {"input", "cache_write", "cache_read", "output"}, "models": {model: usd}}` over the same deduplicated set.
- CLI: `transcript.py cost (--worktree W | --machine | --file F ...) [--since TS] [--until TS]` → JSON of `cost`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing tests:**

  - `test_no_split_priced_as_1h` (`no_split.jsonl`, opus, `cache_creation_input_tokens` 1000 → $0.008);

#### 8c.3 — Task 3: the rate from the sensor

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/limits.py`, `prorab/tests/test_limits.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Readings are monitor-log entries (Task 7): `ts`, `source`, `five_hour`, `seven_day`, `resets_at` (the 5h window's) and `seven_day_resets_at` (written from 8c.7; older lines without it give the week no interval).
- `rate_intervals(readings: list[dict], window: str, d: dict) -> list[tuple[float, float, float]]` — `window` ∈ `five_hour`, `seven_day`; only readings with `source == "sensor"` and both a percentage and a reset time for that window count (status-line readings of idle panes are frozen and would distort the rise), in time order. The first such reading is the anchor; a later reading of the same window (reset times within the sensor's 10-minute tolerance) closes an interval `(t0, t1, rise_pp)` when it is at least `schedule.burn_min_span_s` after the anchor and the percentage rose by at least `rate.min_rise_pp` (below that, rounding dominates), and becomes the next anchor; a reading of another window (a reset) or with a lower percentage becomes the new anchor; any other reading is skipped and the anchor stays. Never an interval across a reset.
- `estimate_rate(readings, spend_between, d: dict, now: float, window: str = "five_hour") -> dict` — `spend_between(t0, t1) -> float` gives the dollars of all sessions on the machine in `[t0, t1)`. Intervals counted: for `five_hour` those ending within `rate.window_horizon_s` before `now`; for `seven_day` those of the current weekly window (the latest `seven_day_resets_at`, 10-minute tolerance). Returns `{"window", "usd_per_pp", "source": "estimated" | "default" | "unknown", "intervals", "usd", "pp", "at": now}` with `usd_per_pp = Σusd / Σpp`; without an interval: `five_hour` → `rate.default_window_usd_per_pp`, `"default"`; `seven_day` → `rate.default_week_usd_per_pp` (null) → `None`, `"unknown"` — never 0, never guessed.
- `rates(readings, d, now, home: str | None = None) -> dict` — `{"five_hour": ..., "seven_day": ...}`; builds one `transcript.spend_series(transcript.machine_files(since=<earliest interval start>, home), d, since=...)` and sums it with `bisect` per interval — one pass over the files per estimate. The machine's spend is all transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/`, the customer's own sessions included: they draw on the same limits.
- `to_pp(usd: float, rate: dict) -> float | None` — `usd / rate["usd_per_pp"]`; `None` when the rate is unknown.
- CLI `limits.py rate [--log F]` → JSON of `rates` over `scheduler.readings` (imported lazily).

  - `test_rate_same_window_only` (a reset between two readings → no interval across it);
  - `test_rate_interval_needs_span_and_rise` (9 min apart → none; 12 min with +0.5 pp → none; 12 min with +2 pp → one);
  - `test_rate_reset_tolerance` (`resets_at` 5 min apart → the same window);
  - `test_rate_ignores_statusline_readings` (only `source: statusline` → the window's default, the week unknown);
  - `test_estimate_rate_sum_over_sum` ($10 over 2 pp and $30 over 4 pp → 6.67 $/pp, 2 intervals, `"estimated"`) — Review Focus 14;
  - `test_rate_window_horizon` (an interval that ended 6 h before `now` is not counted);
  - `test_rate_week_current_window_only` (intervals of the previous weekly window ignored; none in the current one → `None`, `"unknown"`);
  - `test_rate_defaults` (no readings → `five_hour` 5.0 `"default"`, `seven_day` `None` `"unknown"`);
  - `test_to_pp` ($10 at 5 $/pp → 2.0; an unknown rate → `None`);
  - `test_rates_reads_machine_spend` (temp `HOME` with two messages inside one interval → the interval's dollars are their cost).
- [ ] **Step 2:** Run `python3 -m unittest prorab.tests.test_limits -v` → FAIL. **Step 3:** Implement. **Step 4:** Run all tests → PASS.

#### 8c.4 — Task 5: journal (estimates, budget and spend in dollars)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/journal.py`, `prorab/tests/test_journal.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `create_work_order(site, title, body, estimate, goal=None)` — `estimate` must hold `usd` > 0 (else `ValueError`) and may hold `heavy_runs_per_stage` (int > 0); any other key → `ValueError`; stored as kv `estimate.<id>.usd`, `estimate.<id>.heavy_runs_per_stage`. CLI `journal.py work-order SITE TITLE --estimate-usd X [--heavy-runs N] [--goal G] [--body B]` (replaces `--estimate k=v`).
- **Existing callers.** Today only `test_journal.py` creates work orders: its calls with an empty estimate `{}` go through a helper `new_work_order(title="T", goal=None, usd=10)` that passes `{"usd": 10}`; the two CLI tests (`test_cli_work_order_ready_and_lock`, `test_cli_stage_override`) pass `--estimate-usd 10` instead of `--estimate k=v` or no estimate; the free-key estimate of `test_work_order_and_stage_ready_order` leaves that test and becomes `test_work_order_estimate_usd`. Since a script-made work order always has `usd`, a missing estimate later (8c.6 `no_estimate`, Task 9's preflight) is a defensive case: a work order written by hand with `bd`.
- `estimate(work_order: str, kv: dict | None = None) -> dict` — `{"usd": float | None, "heavy_runs_per_stage": int}` (default `machine.heavy_runs_per_stage`).
- `budget(kv=None) -> dict` — `{"usd": float, "week_pp": float}`: the defaults overlaid with kv `budget.usd`, `budget.week_pp` (floats now). `set_budget(**kw)` refuses unknown keys. The customer changes it in words (the foreman writes the kv) or directly with `bd kv`.
- `begin_shift(week_pct, foreman_session: str | None = None)` — as before, plus kv `shift.started_at` (unix time) and `shift.foreman_sessions` (comma list, starting with `foreman_session` when given); resets `shift.spent.*` (`usd` and the counts); and kv `shift.closed_before` (R44) — a comma list of the work orders (issue records that carry a `worktree`) whose work-order bead was closed before `shift.started_at`, by the bead's own close time: `bd show <id> --json`, field `closed_at` (`journal.durations` already sorts `bd list` output by that field; the implementer verifies the name against the installed bd), and, if the field is absent, the time of the work order's last `acceptance` act. A closed work order whose close time is unknown (no `closed_at`, no `acceptance` act) is not listed and stays counted in the shift's spend: spend is never hidden (R44). These close times are read here, once per shift, so the monitor's spend recomputation never starts a `bd` process. `add_foreman_session(session_id: str) -> None` — appends; a foreman handover adds the new session and does not reset the shift's spend. CLI `journal.py shift-begin [--week-pct P] [--foreman-session S]`, `journal.py foreman-session S`.
- `research_begin(key: str, estimate_usd: float, session_id: str, run_cwd: str | None = None) -> None` — kv `research.<key>` = JSON `{"estimate_usd", "since", "session", "run_cwd"}`; `research_items(kv=None) -> dict[str, dict]`. Attribution by start time: a subagent file of the foreman session `session` (`<session>/subagents/*.jsonl`) and, for an item registered with `run_cwd` (a `claude -p` run the foreman starts), a session file in that directory's project directories other than the foreman's own sessions, belongs to the latest item registered before the file's first entry.
- `shift_spend(d: dict, now: float | None = None, kv: dict | None = None, home: str | None = None) -> dict` — reads transcripts in its own process (with `kv`, no `bd` process at all): `{"since", "usd", "work_orders": {id: {"usd_shift", "usd_total", "estimate_usd", "over"}}, "research": {key: {"usd", "estimate_usd", "over"}}, "foreman_usd"}`. Work orders: the issue records that carry a `worktree` (8c.5 records it), except those listed in kv `shift.closed_before` (R44: the work-order bead was closed before the shift began) — these are skipped unread, so the cost of a recomputation does not grow with the journal's history. A crew being stopped says nothing here: crews are also stopped on handover and on restart while their work order stays open. For each remaining work order: `usd_shift` = `transcript.cost(transcript.worktree_files(worktree), since=shift start)`, `usd_total` = the same without `since`, `over` = `usd_total > estimate_usd` (the estimate covers the whole work order). A work order closed during the shift stays in the set until the next shift begins; its `estimate:<id>` item, if any, fires once (the `budget` event fires on a change of the set), and the morning report lists it. Research items: their files since the shift began. The foreman: the transcripts of `shift.foreman_sessions` with their subagent files not taken by research items, since the shift began. `usd` = the sum of the three; one `seen` across all of them, so a message counts once, in the first category that holds it (work orders, then research, then the foreman).
- `over_budget(week_pp_now, spend: dict | None = None, kv=None) -> list[str]` — items: `usd` (`spend["usd"] > budget.usd`), `week_pp` (as before), `estimate:<id>` for every work order of `shift_spend` with `over`, `research:<key>` for every research item over its estimate; without `spend` only `week_pp`.
- `set_spent(work_order: str, usd: float) -> None` — kv `spent.<id>.usd` = the latest computed total (written at acceptance, handover and stop, 8c.5; never an increment). `spend(work_order, sessions=0, runs=0, tokens=0)` stays and only counts for the report — never a limit. `shift_spent(kv=None)` adds `usd` (kv `shift.spent.usd`, written by the foreman handover and the morning report).
- `spend_block(spend: dict, rates: dict, work_order: str | None = None, kv=None) -> str` — the English block appended to stage acts and the foreman's handover act (the journal is where the rate is recorded): `Spend:` lines — the work order's dollars against its estimate and in pp of the window (and of the week once its rate is known), the shift's dollars against the budget, the window rate with its source ("estimated over n intervals" or "default"), the week rate or "unknown".
- CLI: `journal.py research KEY --estimate-usd X --session S [--run-cwd C]` — exits 1 with `{"fits": false, "left_usd": L}` and records nothing when `X` exceeds what is left of the shift budget (by `shift_spend`), else records and prints `{"fits": true, "left_usd": L}`; while "ignore limits and finish" holds (`override()` set) the shift budget is lifted (R42), so the refusal is lifted too: the item is recorded with its estimate and the command prints `{"fits": true, "left_usd": L, "override": true}`; `journal.py shift-spend` → JSON of `shift_spend` plus `over_budget`.

  - `test_shift_spend_attributes_by_worktree` (two issued work orders with worktrees, one message each; a message before the shift start counts in `usd_total`, not in `usd_shift`);

#### 8c.5 — Task 6: crew (spend at acceptance and handover, heavy runs per stage)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/scripts/slots.py`, `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`, `prorab/tests/test_slots.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `start` — once the worktree is found: `journal.issue_set(work_order, <same state>, worktree=<path>)`, so the spend stays attributable after the crew is gone.
- `work_order_spend(work_order: str, d: dict | None = None, home: str | None = None) -> dict` — `{"usd", "estimate_usd", "over", "heavy_runs_per_stage", "pp_window", "pp_week", "rates"}`: `transcript.cost(transcript.worktree_files(<worktree of the issue record>))`, `journal.estimate`, `limits.rates` over the monitor log, `limits.to_pp`. CLI `crew.py spend WORK_ORDER`.
- `slots.run` — the fact source for heavy runs: when the command ends it appends one JSON line `{"cwd", "started_at", "ended_at", "exit", "cmd": cmd[0]}` to `<slots_dir>/<kind>.runs.jsonl` (one `write` in append mode; `cwd` as in the owner record of 8b.7). `slots.runs(kind: str, cwd: str, since: float, slots_dir: str | None = None) -> int` — the number of lines whose `cwd` is `cwd` or a path below it (`os.path.realpath`) and whose `started_at` ≥ `since`. Crews still never touch the journal.
- `check_report(text, criteria, files, worktree, base="main", heavy_limit: int | None = None, cleared: bool = False, heavy_counted: int | None = None)` — a new required line `Heavy runs: <n>` (the crew reports its heavy runs; the dollars the foreman computes itself). Problems: the line missing; `n` differs from `heavy_counted` when given (the facts); the larger of `n` and `heavy_counted` (`n` alone when `heavy_counted` is not given) above `heavy_limit` without `cleared`. CLI `crew.py check_report NAME WORK_ORDER [--stage S] [--base main]` passes `journal.estimate(...)["heavy_runs_per_stage"]`, whether the stage carries `clearance:granted`, and `heavy_counted` = `slots.runs("heavy", <worktree>, since=journal.stage_info(S)["started_at"])`. Without `--stage`, or when the stage has no `started_at`, the count cannot be verified from records: the problem `heavy runs not verifiable: <what is missing>`, never the crew's own number (R52); the foreman always passes `--stage`.
- `accept(work_order: str, stage: str, act_text: str) -> dict` — `work_order_spend`, `journal.set_spent`, the act + `journal.spend_block(...)`, then `journal.accept_stage(stage, act)`; returns `{"minutes", "usd", "estimate_usd", "over"}`. `accept` does not check the report: the foreman runs `crew.py check_report NAME WORK_ORDER --stage <stage>` before it, every time (R52). With `over` the foreman stops that work order after its current step and raises a gate (unless "ignore limits and finish" holds, R42). CLI `crew.py accept WORK_ORDER STAGE --act-file F`.
- `complete_handover` — the `handover` act gets the spend block; `journal.set_spent` with the work order's total; the counts become `journal.spend(work_order, sessions=1)` (the predecessor session's dollars are already in the worktree's transcripts).
- `stop` — `journal.set_spent` with the final total; the counts as before.
- `ask_predecessor(work_order, session_id, cwd, question)` — `cwd` must be the work order's worktree or a path below it (else `ValueError`), so the run's transcript is attributed to the work order and its dollars count in the estimate; the run is still counted (`runs=1`, report only).

#### 8c.6 — Task 6b: scheduler (the estimate in pp, checked before issuing)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/scheduler.py`, `prorab/tests/test_scheduler.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `fits(estimate_usd: float, budget_left_usd: float, rates: dict, used_pct: float | None, cap_stop: float, week_rise_pp: float | None, week_budget_pp: float, override: bool = False) -> dict` — `{"pp_window", "pp_week", "budget": bool, "window": bool, "week": bool, "ok": bool}`: `pp_*` by `limits.to_pp`; `budget` = `estimate_usd <= budget_left_usd`; `window` = `used_pct is None or used_pct + pp_window <= cap_stop` (what is left before the threshold in force); `week` = `pp_week is None or week_rise_pp is None or week_rise_pp + pp_week <= week_budget_pp` (an unknown week rate is never guessed: the week is then guarded only by its measured rise); `ok` = all three; all True while "ignore limits and finish" holds (R42). Advisory: the thresholds themselves stay on measured percentages.
- `build_plan(j, d, now, lim, log, load, *, spend=None, rates=None)` — adds `"budget": {"usd", "spent_usd", "left_usd", "week_pp", "week_rise_pp"}` (from `j.budget()`, `j.shift_spend(d, now)` and `shift.week_start`), `"rates"` (`limits.rates` over the log readings) and `"fits": {id: fits(...)}` for every id in `order`; a work order without an estimate → `{"ok": false, "reason": "no_estimate"}` (defensive: since 8c.4 the script refuses a work order without `usd`, so this happens only for a work order written by hand with `bd`). The foreman issues only work orders with `ok`, in `order`; one that does not fit waits for the customer with a gate. `spend` and `rates` are injectable for tests.

#### 8c.7 — Task 7: monitor (the budget event in dollars, the rate in the log)

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/monitor.py`, `prorab/tests/test_monitor.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `deps` gain `shift_spend` (`journal.shift_spend` with the poll's kv snapshot) and `rates` (`limits.rates`); `over_budget` is called as `over_budget(week, spend, kv=...)`.
- **Spend.** Computed on the first poll and then at most every `monitor.spend_every_s` (kept in `mem["spend"]` with its time, persisted like the rest), by reading files inside the monitor's own process — never a process launch: the per-poll process budget of 8b.8 stays exactly two.
- **Event** `budget <item>=<spent>/<limit>,...` in dollars and pp: `usd=361.20/350.00`, `estimate:<id>=31.50/30.00`, `research:<key>=12.40/10.00`, `week_pp=10.5/10`; once per change of the set of exceeded items, as before. None while "ignore limits and finish" holds — the spend is still computed and logged.
- **Log line** adds `seven_day_resets_at` on every poll, and `spend_usd` (the shift's total) and `rate` (the `rates` result: value and source for both windows) when computed this poll; the rate is re-estimated at most every `rate.every_s`, and the log keeps every estimate.

#### 8d.1 — references: `acts.md`

**Files:** Modify `prorab/references/acts.md`, `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`

### Task 10b: Fast, unhangable tests and the stop-A defaults (shift 3)

The foreman's work order for shift 3 ("make the tests fast and unhangable first") and its answers at stop point A. Five sub-tasks, **one commit each**, in this order; the full suite passes after every commit; every new string is English. Measured on this machine: `bd` 1.3.1 is 136 MB and costs about 0.3 s per exec (`bd create` about 0.8 s, `bd init` about 4 s), because the machine's security agents scan every exec; copying a freshly initialised `.beads` (2 MB) takes about 15 ms. Before this task the suite took 20–28 minutes, almost all of it in `test_journal` and `test_shift`. **Targets:** the fast tier under 2 minutes; the full suite under 10 minutes.

#### 10b.1 — a timeout on every bd, herdr and git call

**Files:** Modify `prorab/references/defaults.json`, `prorab/tests/test_defaults.py`, `prorab/scripts/journal.py`, `prorab/scripts/herdr_cli.py`, `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/tests/fakes/herdr` (a `sleep` option per route); Create `prorab/tests/test_timeouts.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `defaults.json`: `timeouts = {"bd_s": 30, "git_s": 60, "herdr_s": 60}`.
- `journal._run` — `subprocess.run(..., timeout=timeouts.bd_s)`; `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` → `JournalError("bd <args> timed out after <n> s")`. The child is killed (`subprocess.run` does so on a timeout); nothing is retried.
- `herdr_cli.run` / `herdr_cli._text` — the default `timeout_s` is `timeouts.herdr_s`; the waits that pass their own bound keep it (`send`, `recover_stalled`, `wait_ready`: `ms // 1000 + 30`); `HerdrError("cli_timeout", "herdr <args> timed out after <n> s")`.
- `crew._git` — `timeout=timeouts.git_s`; a timeout raises `RuntimeError("git <args> timed out after <n> s")`. Every caller keeps its contract for a failing git call: `check_report` turns it into a problem (a refusal, never a pass), and so on for each caller (the report lists them).
- Every other `bd`, `herdr` or `git` subprocess call in `prorab/scripts/` gets the same treatment; the report lists every `subprocess` call site and its bound. Values are read from `defaults.json` at call time, so a test can lower them.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing tests** in `test_timeouts.py` (fast: no real `bd`, no real herdr; a temp dir first on `PATH` holds a fake `bd` and a fake `git` that sleep 10 s; the timeouts lowered to 1 s): `test_bd_timeout_is_an_error` (`journal.bd(["list"])` raises `JournalError` naming the command and "timed out" within 5 s); `test_git_timeout_is_an_error` (`crew._git`); `test_herdr_timeout_is_an_error` (the fake herdr route sleeps; `herdr_cli.run` raises `HerdrError` with code `cli_timeout`); `test_check_report_git_timeout_is_a_refusal` (`ok` False, a problem naming the timeout); `test_defaults.py::test_values_match_spec` gains `timeouts`.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Run → FAIL. **Step 3:** Implement. **Step 4:** Run the full suite → PASS.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** `fix(scripts): a timeout on every bd, herdr and git call`.

#### 10b.4 — a bounded, parallel test runner

**Files:** Create `prorab/tests/run.py`, `prorab/tests/test_run.py`

The foreman's rule for every test command — an upper bound, a log checked at least every 10 minutes, a stop at 20 minutes or after 10 minutes without log growth — moves into the repo, and the bd-backed modules run side by side, so the full suite fits in 10 minutes.

**Interfaces:**
- `python3 prorab/tests/run.py [--fast] [--jobs N] [--bound S] [--stall S] [--log-dir D] [MODULE ...]` — stdlib only. Without modules: every `prorab/tests/test_*.py`. `--fast` sets `PRORAB_FAST=1` for the children. Groups: each bd-backed module (`test_journal`, `test_shift`) is a group of its own; all other modules form one group. At most `--jobs` groups (default 2) run at once, each in a child `python3` that runs its modules with `unittest` at verbosity 2 and prints each test's time, writing to `<log dir>/<group>.log` (default: a new temp directory, printed at the start).
- **Bounds:** a child still running after `--bound` seconds (default 1200), or whose log has not grown for `--stall` seconds (default 600), gets its stack dumped (`faulthandler`, all threads) and is killed; the runner reports `bound` or `stall` for that group. Exit 0 only when every group passed; the summary prints, per group, the tests run, failures, errors, skips and the time, and the slowest ten tests overall.
- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing tests** in `test_run.py` against a synthetic test package in a temp directory (never the real suite: no recursion): `test_groups_split_bd_modules` (the grouping function); `test_bound_kills_a_hung_group` (a test that sleeps 60 s, `--bound 2` → the group reported `bound` with a stack dump in its log, the runner exits non-zero within ~10 s); `test_stall_kills_a_silent_group` (`--stall 2`); `test_summary_and_exit_code` (one failing test → exit 1; all passing → exit 0).

### Task 12c: the week by pace (amendment W)

> Done (`e43c9f6`). Amendment F replaces this task's projection formula, its 24-hour burn horizon, its reserve and its report lines (R101, Task 13b.7); its levels, fallback, notices, `week.pause` and `fits.pace` stand.

Amendment W (the customer, 2026-10-22): the week limit is use-it-or-lose-it, and fixed week thresholds (notify at 50 %, clearance only at 70 %) ignore how much time is left before the reset — in the customer's words, roughly, "it is not that bad: half of the week is left, but only two working days are". The week is now judged by its projection at the reset (spec "The week by pace to its reset"; rulings R84–R88 in the header; the author's choices on what they left open are marked "author's choice" below). Runs after stop point B and before Task 13, once the documents commit of amendment W (the spec and this plan) has been reviewed. TDD, **one commit**; the full suite passes before it; every new string is English. The pressure scenarios that mention the week are rerun by the controller after the commit, not in this task.

**Files:**
- Modify: `prorab/references/defaults.json`, `prorab/scripts/limits.py`, `prorab/scripts/scheduler.py`, `prorab/scripts/monitor.py`, `prorab/scripts/shift.py`, `prorab/SKILL.md` (one line and an equal cut, Step 3b), `prorab/evals/pressure/scenarios.md` (the "Week" row of the values table, Step 3b), `prorab/tests/test_defaults.py`, `prorab/tests/test_limits.py`, `prorab/tests/test_scheduler.py`, `prorab/tests/test_monitor.py`, `prorab/tests/test_shift.py`

**Keys** (values verbatim):
- New top-level section `week`, after `limits`: `{"alert_pct": 95, "clearance_pct": 100, "margin_pp": 20, "fallback_notify_pct": 50, "fallback_clearance_pct": 70, "burn_horizon_s": 86400, "reserve_pp_per_hour": 1.0, "calendar": {"days": ["mon", "tue", "wed", "thu", "fri"], "start": "09:00", "end": "19:00"}}` — the levels (R85), the fallback (R86), the burn's horizon (author's choice: 24 h, one whole day of the customer's cycle — working hours and a night — so that neither a burst nor a quiet hour alone sets the pace for the days to the reset), the reserve per working hour and the working calendar (R84; author's choice: in `week`, next to the only rule that reads them). `days` are names in `date.weekday()` order (`mon` … `sun`); `start` and `end` are local wall-clock `HH:MM`, `start` before `end`; the zone is the machine's (the customer's), so there is no zone key.
- Removed: `limits.seven_day` (`notify` 50, `clearance_only` 70) — the fixed thresholds live on only as `week.fallback_notify_pct` and `week.fallback_clearance_pct`, one place per number. `monitor._limit_state` is its only reader today.
- Unchanged: `limits.week_unknown_blind_s` 1800 (the unknown week, R86); `budget.week_pp` 10 (R87); `rate.default_week_usd_per_pp` 10.0 (the $/pp rate is not the burn); `schedule.burn_min_span_s` 600 and `rate.min_rise_pp` 1 (the interval discipline the burn shares with the rate); `rate.every_s` 600 (also the burn's cadence in the monitor).

**Interfaces:**
- What a limits reading carries today: `limits.read_sensor` already returns `seven_day_resets_at` (the latest `seven_day.resets_at` of the fresh files, 10-minute tolerance), and every monitor-log line carries it since 8c.7; the status-line paths (`parse_statusline`, `_from_panes`) leave it `None`. Nothing is added there (author's choice): the status line's week countdown stays unparsed, since without the sensor there is no burn and the fallback applies anyway.
- `limits.week_burn(readings: list[dict], d: dict, now: float) -> dict` — the week's burn from monitor-log readings. The current week: the latest `seven_day_resets_at` among `_sensor_points(readings, "seven_day")` (sensor readings with both a week percentage and a week reset), with the 10-minute tolerance, and only when it lies after `now` (a week whose reset has passed is not current). The intervals: `rate_intervals` over the current week's readings only (8c.3, unchanged: at least `schedule.burn_min_span_s` and `rate.min_rise_pp` apart, a drop re-anchors, never across a reset), counted when the interval ends within `week.burn_horizon_s` before `now`. `pp` = the sum of their rises; `hours` = from the start of the earliest counted interval to the latest sensor reading of the current week at or before `now` (author's choice: a quiet stretch since the last interval lowers the burn instead of leaving the last busy interval in force; its rise below `rate.min_rise_pp` is not counted). Returns `{"pp_per_h": pp / hours | None, "source": "measured" | "none", "reason": None | "no sensor reading of the current week" | "no interval within the horizon", "intervals": n, "pp": float, "hours": float, "resets_at": <the current week's reset> | None, "at": now}` — without an interval `pp_per_h` is `None`, never 0. It reads no file itself.
- `limits.working_hours(start: float, end: float, calendar: dict, tz=None) -> float` — the hours of the working calendar in `[start, end)`: for every local date from `start`'s to `end`'s whose weekday is in `calendar["days"]`, the overlap of `[start, end)` with that date's `calendar["start"]`–`calendar["end"]`, each bound built from that date's local wall time, so a daylight-saving change never shifts a working day; `tz` `None` = the machine's local zone, a `tzinfo` in tests; `end <= start` → 0.0; an empty `days` → 0.0; a calendar whose `start` is not before its `end` → `ValueError`.
- `limits.WEEK_LEVELS` = `{"unknown": 0, "room": 0, "normal": 0, "notify": 1, "no_new_mass": 2, "clearance_only": 3}`.
- `limits.week_projection(lim: dict, burn: dict | None, d: dict, now: float, tz=None) -> dict` — R84–R86. Every key of `lim` and `burn` is read with `.get`, a missing key counting as `None`: the limits dicts of the existing `build_plan` tests carry no `seven_day_resets_at`, and the monitor can hold `lim = {}`. `burn` `None` (the monitor before its first computation) reads as no measured burn and no reset time.
  - `used` = `lim.get("seven_day")`. `None` → `basis` `"unknown"`, `allows` `"unknown"`, every number `None`: nothing is projected, and the unknown-week rules stay as they are (`blind_week`; `cap_for(..., week_known=False)` at night).
  - `resets_at` = `lim.get("seven_day_resets_at")` when it lies after `now`, else `burn.get("resets_at")` when it lies after `now` (author's choice: when the status line took the place of a stale sensor, the week keeps its reset time, which never moves within a week — this also keeps the basis from flickering), else `None`. With a reset time: `hours_to_reset` = `(resets_at − now) / 3600`, `working_hours` = `working_hours(now, resets_at, d["week"]["calendar"], tz)`, `reserve_pp` = `working_hours × week.reserve_pp_per_hour`; without one, all three `None`.
  - With a reset time and `burn.get("pp_per_h")` not `None`: `basis` `"projection"`, `projected` = `used + pp_per_h × hours_to_reset + reserve_pp`; `allows` = `"clearance_only"` when `projected ≥ week.clearance_pct`, else `"no_new_mass"` when `≥ week.alert_pct`, else `"room"` when `≤ 100 − week.margin_pp`, else `"normal"`.
  - Otherwise: `basis` `"fallback"`, `projected` `None`, `reason` `"reset time unknown"` (no reset time) or `"no measured burn"`; `allows` = `"clearance_only"` when `used ≥ week.fallback_clearance_pct`, else `"notify"` when `≥ week.fallback_notify_pct`, else `"normal"` — never `"room"`.
  - Returns `{"used", "burn": <the burn dict>, "resets_at", "hours_to_reset", "working_hours", "reserve_pp_per_hour", "reserve_pp", "projected", "basis", "reason", "allows", "level": WEEK_LEVELS[allows]}`.
- CLI `limits.py week [--log F] [--now TS]` → JSON of `week_projection(get_limits(<pane texts, read only when the sensor gives nothing>, d, now), week_burn(scheduler.readings(F), d, now), d, now)`; `scheduler` imported lazily, as for `rate`. Task 13 check 16 runs it.
- `scheduler.fits(estimate_usd, budget_left_usd, rates, used_pct, cap_stop, week_rise_pp, week_budget_pp, override=False, week_hold=False)` — adds `"pace": not week_hold`; `ok` = `budget and window and week and pace`; all four True while "ignore limits and finish" holds (R86). `week` stays the shift budget's pp check (R87).
- `scheduler.cleared(j, work_order: str) -> bool` — True when the work-order bead or its current stage (the first of `j.open_stages(work_order)`) carries `clearance:granted` (`j.labels`): the spec's "work order with a clearance" (author's choice: a clearance is advance permission from the customer, so it passes both levels).
- `scheduler.build_plan(j, d, now, lim, log, load, *, spend=None, rates=None, week_burn=None)` — `week_burn` injectable for tests (default `limits.week_burn(readings(log), d, now)`). Without a log (a missing file reads as `[]`) the burn's `pp_per_h` and `resets_at` are `None`, with the reason "no sensor reading of the current week", so the plan's `week` is the fallback when the week percentage is known and `unknown` when it is not (R86). The existing `build_plan` tests stay as they are: their `lim` carries no `seven_day_resets_at` and their log path does not exist, so their week is the fallback (at 20 % or 25 %: `normal`) or `unknown` (`None`), nothing is held and no `labels` call is made. The plan gains `"week"`: `limits.week_projection(lim, week_burn, d, now)` plus `"pause"`, the names of the registered crews (`j.crews()`) whose work order (kv `crew.<name>.work_order`) is not `cleared` — only when `allows` is `"clearance_only"` and "ignore limits and finish" does not hold, else `[]` (author's choice: these crews stand with a gate instead of resuming by themselves, so the projection does not swing them on and off). Every `fits` call passes `week_hold` = `allows` in (`"no_new_mass"`, `"clearance_only"`) and not `cleared(j, id)`, and `False` while the command holds (author's choice: at the alert the crews at work go on; only new work orders without a clearance wait, without a gate, for a later plan). `cleared` is called only at those two levels, so an ordinary plan makes no extra `bd` call. `cap_for` and `allowed_parallel` stay as they are (author's choice): `cap_for`'s only week input is `week_known`, the unknown-week rule R86 keeps, and a clearance passes the week's levels per work order, which one `no_new_mass` number cannot express; the week adds no bound, and `"room"` changes no number — the foreman may fill `new`, never exceed it.
- `monitor.poll`:
  - `deps` gain `week_burn` (`limits.week_burn(readings(), d, now)`), computed with the rate — at most every `rate.every_s`, kept in `mem["week_burn"]` with its time and persisted like the rest — by reading the log in-process, so the per-poll process budget of 8b.8 stays exactly two; the log line gains `week_burn` on the polls that compute it.
  - On every poll with limits: `proj = limits.week_projection(lim, <the cached burn, or None before the first computation>, d, now)`. The week has one mechanism only, the week notice, and it leaves `_limit_state`: `_limit_state(lim, cap)` (the `d` parameter goes, it was read only for `limits.seven_day.notify`) returns `[band, reached]`, the 5h band and `reached`, which keep their rule (fire when worse; a fall re-arms); a persisted `mem["limit"]` of another length (the old three-slot form) reads as `[0, False]`.
  - The week notice fires once per level per week (R85: one notice; author's choice, since the projection, unlike a percentage, can fall and rise again within a window). `mem["week_notice"]` = `{"resets_at", "level"}` holds the highest week level reported for the current week, and the week is keyed by `proj["resets_at"]` — the projection's reset, which falls back on the logged week reset when the sensor is stale — never by `lim`'s, so a stale sensor does not start a new week. A known `proj["resets_at"]` more than 10 minutes from a known stored one starts a new week at level 0; an unknown one keeps the stored week; a stored unknown one takes the first known one without starting a new week. `proj["level"]` above the stored level fires the notice and becomes the new maximum; a drop never re-arms it within the week.
  - One `limit` line per poll, as today, when the 5h state gets worse or the week notice fires. The line ends with ` week:<allows>` only when the week notice fires on that poll — `limit 20 50 sensor day week:notify`, `limit 30 62 sensor day week:no_new_mass` — so a `limit` raised by the 5h window alone carries no week field and never repeats a week notice.
  - While "ignore limits and finish" holds: no `limit` event at all, as before, and `mem["week_notice"]` is left as it was, so a level reached under the command is reported once the command ends; readings and the burn are still logged.
- `shift.report` — in the "Limits" section the line `- week: …` becomes the week's lines (R88), from the same limits as the other lines and `limits.week_burn(scheduler.readings(), d, now)`:
  - `- week: 50 % used, resets 2026-10-23 19:00 (in 48.0 h)` (`_clock`; `reset time unknown` without one);
  - `- week burn: 0.30 pp/h (measured over 22.5 h, 5 intervals)`, or `- week burn: none — fallback thresholds (no measured burn)`, with the projection's `reason`;
  - `- customer's reserve: 20 working hours left × 1 pp/h (default; calendar mon–fri 09:00–19:00) = 20 pp`, or `- customer's reserve: unknown (reset time unknown)`;
  - `- week projection at the reset: 50 + 0.30 × 48.0 + 20 = 84.4 %`, or `- week projection: none — fallback thresholds 50 % notify, 70 % clearance only`;
  - `- the week allows: normal` | `room for more (the foreman may propose a night or weekend shift)` | `no new mass runs` | `clearance only` | `notify the customer (fallback threshold)`;
  - an unknown week: the single line `- week: unknown (no projection; the unknown-week rules apply)`.
  Every threshold, the reserve per hour and the calendar are rendered from `week.*`, never written into the code. Numbers by `_g` (`%g` after rounding to two places), the burn with two decimals, the hours and the projection with one. Nothing else in the report changes: "Week rise" against `budget.week_pp` under "Budget" stays (R87).

### Task 13b: fixes from live run 2 (amendment F)

The second live run of Task 13 (22 October; Claude Code 2.1.307, herdr 0.9.5) found eight defects, F1–F8; the foreman ordered them fixed through the normal implement–review loop before the crew hooks of Task 14 and a short third live run (R99–R101 in the header). Eight sub-steps, **one code commit each** (F8's items share one), in the order F1, F2, F3, F5, F7, F6, F4, F8, after the documents commit of amendment F is reviewed; each starts with its failing tests; the full suite (`python3 prorab/tests/run.py`) passes before each commit, and so do `python3 -m unittest prorab.tests.test_language prorab.tests.test_no_private_data` (one skip without the word list). Every new string is English; fixtures are synthetic (neutral paths such as `/home/user/projects/demo-app`, no pane ids or session ids of the live run). The last sub-step, 13b.9, is the third live run: it runs after the commits of Task 14 and Task 14b, since it also checks the emergency stop and the hooks (Task 14 Step 6) and `consent_done` (Task 14b, R105).

**SKILL.md** (R100): a sub-step that changes `prorab/SKILL.md` names the change by reference only — what the sentence must say and what may be cut to pay for it. The opus drafter writes the text after this plan's review, cumulatively (one draft per code commit that touches SKILL.md); the implementer applies it verbatim. Each draft keeps the whole file at most 12 000 characters (`python3 -c "print(len(open('prorab/SKILL.md', encoding='utf-8').read()))"`; 11 997 at the start of Task 13b), the frontmatter and the section "Safety rules" byte-identical to the spec's (the spec's Safety rules changed one sentence in amendment F's documents commit, so 13b.1's draft — the first — carries that change), and every Task 12 Step 1 item. The pressure scenarios the sub-step names (`prorab/evals/pressure/scenarios.md`), plus any scenario whose rule the cut touched, are rerun in one GREEN round 5 on the final SKILL.md after Task 14b's commit (R104, R105), and recorded in `after.md` in a commit of its own.

**Dependency** (author's choice): F2 and F3 need a herdr call that takes any answer on exit 0 as success (`pane close`, `pane send-keys`), and F5's root cause is exactly that call missing. So 13b.2 introduces `herdr_cli.run_ack`, 13b.3 uses it, and 13b.4 (F5) applies it to the root cause and to every other call whose answer the scripts do not read. The order F1 … F8 stays.

#### 13b.1 — F1: trust by the exact site path

Live: `claude -w` refused the toy site with ``Error creating worktree: Workspace trust not yet accepted. Run `claude` once in this directory and accept the trust dialog, then retry with --worktree.`` although its parent folders were trusted in `~/.claude.json`; the site's own path had no entry. Once the customer had accepted the trust in the site itself, fresh `-w` worktrees inside it asked nothing.

**Files:** Modify `prorab/scripts/crew.py`, `prorab/references/defaults.json` (`crew.trust_path`, `crew.trust_retry_s`), `prorab/tests/test_crew.py`, `prorab/tests/test_defaults.py`, `prorab/SKILL.md` (Step 3b)

**Interfaces:**
- `defaults.json`: `crew.trust_path` = `"~/.claude.json"` (expanded from `HOME` at call time, like every `_path` key), `crew.trust_retry_s` = `1`.
- `crew.site_trust(site_path: str) -> bool | None` — reads `crew.trust_path` read only: never written, never copied, and no value but the one flag is read into a result, a log line or an error message. `True` when `projects[<key>]["hasTrustDialogAccepted"] is True` for the site path as given (a trailing `/` stripped) or for `os.path.realpath(site_path)` (author's choice: either key counts, since macOS shows `/var/…` as `/private/var/…` and Claude Code keys the file by the path it saw); `False` when the file is missing, `projects` holds neither key, or the flag is not `True` — a parent folder's entry never counts. A file that does not parse (Claude Code rewrites it often): sleep `crew.trust_retry_s`, read once more; still unreadable → `None` (author's choice: unknown is not "untrusted", and the screen refusal stays the backstop).
- `crew.start` — after its existing checks (name, issue record, `parallel_limit`, `files_overlap`) and before any `agent start`: `site_trust(site_path)` `False` → the start takes its pane (a split as today; 13b.2 makes it reusable), records `journal.issue_set(work_order, "not_issued", name=name, pane=pane, reason="needs_trust")` and returns `{"status": "needs_trust", "pane_id": pane, "site": site_path, "trust": "missing"}` — no `agent start`, no keys, no session id spent. `None` → the start goes on and its result carries `"trust": "unknown"`; `True` → unchanged.
- The screen backstop is already in place: `workspace trust not yet accepted` is a `trust` marker in `herdr_cli.CONSENT_KINDS`, and `test_start_timeout_with_worktree_refusal_needs_trust` covers it on `fixtures/consent/worktree_trust_refused.txt`; both stay (author's choice: the consent markers stay in that one catalogue rather than in `defaults.json`).
- `CrewBase` in `test_crew.py` writes a synthetic `~/.claude.json` under its temp `HOME` that trusts the test site, so every existing start test keeps its path; the new tests replace that file.
